匿名审稿意见
稿件： Minimal preimages of the Fibonacci rank map: squarefree fibers and weighted covers
编辑建议： 拒稿，但明确允许在实质性重构后重新投稿（reject and resubmit，而非普通大修）。
总体评价
稿件包含一个具有发表价值的数学核心：将精确秩纤维 Bn​=α−1(n) 的整除极小元归约为带“降阶见证”的不可约 lcm-覆盖，并由此识别平方自由指标上的规范平方自由切片及其加权计数。我没有在主文中发现足以断言该核心分类为假的反例。
但是，现稿尚不能接受，原因并非期刊层级不足，而是以下两项结构性缺陷：


**送审文件不是一份可独立核验的完整证明。**定理 5.1 和定理 5.10 的上界部分明确调用仅存在于未附补编中的 prime–ladder alternative 与 prime-power lifting 结果；这两处直接支撑第 4、5 项主张，而不是可选的技术补充。 


**现行优先权叙事遗漏了最接近结构主定理的先行工作：Carl G. Wagner 1978 年的 “Minimal multiplicative covers of an integer”。**该文直接研究满足
lcm(d1​,…,dr​)=n,lcm(d1​,…,di​​,…,dr​)<n
的整除元组，并明确将其视为有限集极小覆盖的乘法推广。这与本文平方自由／素原子部分的组合骨架高度重合。Mathematics+1


只要这两项得到彻底处理，我认为修改后的核心成果原则上达到《The Fibonacci Quarterly》的发表门槛；现状则不达到。

一、数学正确性
1. 见证覆盖分类与 ω(m)≤ω(n)
判断：主文中的证明成立。
Lemma 4.3 正确地把整除极小性化为逐个素坐标降低一次后的 lcm 严格下降。这里检验 m/pi​ 已足够：任一真因子至少在一个素坐标上不超过 ei​−1，而秩对整除关系单调。Lemma 4.5 对满指数坐标逐坐标比较，准确刻画了降低原子后何时仍保持总 lcm=n。Theorem 4.6 随后给出的双射及由唯一分解得到的逆映射没有缺口；私人坐标也确实给出原子集到 P(n) 的单射。 
这里需要修改的是优先权定位而非证明本身。见证覆盖定理不应继续表述成首次发现的不可约 lcm-覆盖机制；其新部分是：


将 Wagner 型删除一个因子的条件推广为把一个 prime-power rank label d(θ) 降到 d−(θ)；


证明 Fibonacci prime-power arithmetic 只产生素原子与梯级原子；


将抽象覆盖的算术可实现性与 Mn​ 唯一对应。


**消除异议所需修改：**增加一个专门的比较命题，明确说明在所有原子均为素数、因而 d−(θ)=1 时，本文条件退化为 Wagner 的 minimal multiplicative cover；再说明梯级原子是本文真正新增的“lowered-label”结构。

2. 素数逆向射线及两个动力学推论
判断：论证正确，但原创性等级显著低于现稿标题化处理所暗示的等级。
Theorem 3.1 的迭代是有效的。初始 y≥7、y=12 有精确秩素数；以后每个前驱都是不小于 7 的素数，故不会落入例外秩 6,12。若射线中有重复素数，就产生周期点；结合固定点分类和无非平凡周期可排除。最后
pr​∣Fpr−1​​,pr​≤Fpr−1​​<φpr−1​
给出所声明的迭代指数上界。x=12 时采用 7↦8↦6↦12 的桥接也正确。
FitzGibbons–Javaheri–Miller–Verga 的正式问题确实分别询问：


每个 k≥1 是否存在无穷多个两两互素、固定点阶为 k 的整数；


每个固定点 x>5 的吸引域是否含无穷多个两两互素元素。Taylor & Francis Online+1


因此，从逻辑结论看，本文得到无穷多个不同素数，确实同时回答了两个问题，而且所得结论强于“两两互素整数”。正文第 459–464 行的实质判断是正确的。
但从独立新结果的门槛看，我的裁决是：


它达到“新的、值得记录的推论或应用”的门槛；


它不达到“独立的素数存在性定理”或本论文首要数学创新的门槛；


它不能单独支撑本稿发表，因为证明只是逐次调用进口的精确秩素数存在定理，并未建立新的素数构造、分布或筛法机制。


“strictly strengthen and resolve”在形式逻辑上可以辩护，但在摘要和引言中容易造成原创机制上的过度印象。
**消除异议所需修改：**将措辞改为：

“As a direct corollary of classical exact-rank prime existence, we answer both questions affirmatively in the stronger prime form.”

并将该部分降为较短的应用节，明确写出“no new prime-existence input is proved here”。不必删除该结果，但不应让它先于见证覆盖定理成为摘要中的主要卖点。

3. 秩纯部分、例外支撑、平方自由切片及极小横截模型
判断：主文中的核心论证成立。
Theorem 5.4 对例外支撑的分析正确：nS​=2,6,12 所对应支撑至多两个，且基数至多二；不同支撑给出不同的 nS​，故所选择的精确秩素数也不同。对非例外不可约覆盖，每个素原子满足
En​(pS​)=Tn​(pS​)=S,
因此不可约覆盖条件与见证覆盖条件完全一致，唯一性由整数唯一分解和 α(pS​)=nS​ 恢复。
平方自由指标上的反向包含也正确：若 m∈Mn​ 平方自由，则其全部原子均为素原子；因 n 平方自由，任一秩 d(p)∣n 必等于对应满支撑的 nS​，从而落入所构造的秩纯部分。平方自由精确纤维中的极小性与全纤维中的极小性一致，因为平方自由数的所有因子仍平方自由。
这里真正有实质新意的部分是：


加权和对一个标准精确纤维切片的精确计数；


平方自由指标上的规范性识别；


Fibonacci 精确秩素数对经典不可约覆盖的算术实现。


Corollary 5.5 的 minimal-transversal 表述本身是标准超图对偶的应用，应继续作为解释性重述，而不应列为独立组合突破。
**消除异议所需修改：**增加至少一个完整手算实例，分别展示：


一个无例外的奇平方自由层；


一个含 2,6 或 12 例外支撑的偶数层；


n=91 中 169 位于非秩纯部分的边界现象。


现有抽象证明正确，但对于该刊读者，缺少实例会显著降低可读性和可核查性。

4. #Mn​ 的上下界及平均界
判断：下界和粗平均界可跟随；精细上界在当前送审材料中不可核验。
非空性证明正确：对 n≥3，严格递增性给出 α(Fn​)=n，故有限偏序集 Bn​ 有极小元。私人坐标构造的下界也成立：选取避开坐标 2,3 的 r=⌊k/2⌋ 个私人坐标，保证每个构造秩含一个不在 {2,3} 中的素坐标，因而避开精确秩素数存在定理的例外。不同映射 H 产生不同支撑族及不同乘积。
问题出现在上界。证明依赖以下未在主文证明的断言：


任一有效原子族或者是若干精确秩素数的并，或者是至多含一个元素的梯级族；


非空本质支撑只能满足 I=J 或 ∣I∣=1。


这些结论被指向未附补编，而随后正是它们给出每个私人坐标至多
2R(n)2k−s
种选择。
因此，当前不能认证：


第 4 项中的精细上界；


由该上界导出的 (log2/4)k2+klogR(n)+O(k)；


所有依赖该上界的条件渐近结论。


粗界 L(n)≤ω(n)logn、Wigert 型包络和平均上界不受此缺失影响。
**消除异议所需修改：**以下两种方案任选其一：


将完整的 prime-power rank-lifting 公式、prime–ladder dichotomy、原子族有限性及“每个梯级槽至多一个候选”的证明移入正文或正式附录；


将完整补编作为同一投稿包的可审稿组成部分，并在正文中使用稳定的定理编号，而非“Supplement, section ‘Low-support classifications’”这类不稳定指引。


若不提供这些证明，则必须删除精细上界、定理 5.1 及所有由 R(n) 推出的结论；这会实质性削弱论文，不能以“标准事实”一句代替。

5. 秩窗去聚合与 fibotomic 熵界
5.1 秩窗去聚合
判断：推导在 prime–ladder 结论成立的前提下可信，但当前同样不完整。
窗口基数
#WJ​(n)=ℓ∈/J∏​νℓ​(n)
及
0≤logR(n)−logA∗(n)≤log2+ℓe∥n∑​loge
之后的均值估计没有明显错误。双重级数
p∑​j≥2∑​pjlogj​
绝对收敛，足以给出 O(xloglogx)。然而关键夹逼
P(n)≤R(n)≤P(n)+1
仍依赖未附的梯级原子分类。
**消除异议所需修改：**与上一项相同；缺少 prime–ladder 定理时，Theorem 5.1 不可保留为已证结果。
5.2 Fibotomic rank entropy
判断：证明思路成立，未发现反例；但文献归属和若干中间断言需写得更自足。
由 fibotomic 分解得到
Fd​=Ψd​(1)∈N,logFd​=φ(d)logφ+O(1),
精确秩素数的乘积整除 Fd​，再结合
p≡±1(modd)
对第 i 个精确秩素数给出下界，最后用 Stirling 公式得到系数 logφ/2。这一链条在数学上是一致的。
当前仍须补足：


明确证明带负 Möbius 指数的表达式为何等于正整数 Ψd​(1)；


单独陈述
α(p)=d∏​p∣Fd​
的引理，并说明不会因 Möbius 商式而发生约消；


将 d=3,5 以及空精确秩层 d=6,12 的处理统一列出；


在“一致 o(1)”处明确量词，而不是仅以二分情形隐含完成。


这些是可修复的写作缺口，不是我所发现的数学错误。

6. 固定总质量下的加权不可约覆盖极值
判断：命题及等号分类正确。
展开 wS​=1+xS​ 后，
S∈C∏​(1+xS​)≥1+S∈C∑​xS​
并对全部不可约覆盖求和。每个非空支撑至少出现一次，而全支撑只出现在单元素覆盖中，故得到锐界。对 k≥3，每个真非空支撑至少出现在两个不可约覆盖中，因此等号迫使全部真支撑的超额质量为零。
该结果是一个正确而有用的支持性引理，但证明十分初等，不宜作为论文独立主要创新。其价值在于精确说明“仅知 ω(Fn​) 的总质量不足以控制加权覆盖和”，而不是建立新的深层极值理论。

7. H1、H2 与 BLMS 猜想的关系尚未陈述完整
现稿只指出 BLMS 猜想会迫使 H2 失败。事实上，稿件自己的估计还推出 H1 也会失败。
在 BLMS 猜想下，对几乎所有平方自由合数 n，稿件已得到
logR(n)≥loglogn−ω(n)log2+O(1).
再利用平方自由整数上的正常阶
ω(n)=(1+o(1))loglogn,
可得
ω(n)logR(n)​≥1−log2+o(1)
在一个正密度集合上成立。因此，对任意
0<ε<1−log2,
H1 中的异常集不可能是 o(x)。 BLMS 原文确实猜测复合指标上 ω(Fn​)≫logn。Samir Siksek
这不使任何无条件定理失效，也不使“H1 蕴含条件正常阶”这一逻辑命题失效；但把 H1、H2 无限定地命名为“Conjecture”会隐去它们与一项已发表猜想的直接冲突。
消除异议所需修改：


最稳妥的方案是改称 sufficient hypotheses；


若作者坚持将 H1、H2 作为自己的反向猜想，则必须明确写出：BLMS 猜想将同时否定 H1 和 H2；


Corollary 5.14 应表述为纯条件推论，不应暗示这些条件目前有正面算术证据。



二、新颖性与优先权
1. Wagner（1978）是必须补入的最近结构先行工作
这是本报告最重要的优先权意见。
Wagner 的 Minimal multiplicative covers of an integer 并非仅仅讨论普通集合覆盖；其定义直接是由 n 的因子组成的元组，整体最小公倍数为 n，而删除任一分量后最小公倍数严格小于 n。他还明确指出，在 n 为不同素数乘积时，该问题推广了有限集的极小覆盖枚举。Mathematics+1
与本文的精确关系如下：


在平方自由极小元或一般秩纯素原子部分，d−(p)=1，本文见证条件正是 Wagner 条件的无序版本，再附加精确秩素数的算术选择；


对梯级原子，本文把“删除 di​”推广为“将 di​ 降到 di−​”；


本文的新增内容是这种降低操作的 Fibonacci prime-power 实现、唯一原子分解、平方自由规范切片以及精确秩乘数权重。


因此，现稿仍可能具有新意，但不能再将其表述为首次把精确纤维极小元归约为不可约 lcm-覆盖而不讨论 Wagner。
足以消除异议的修改：


在引言中增加 Wagner 1978 的独立小节；


给出上述退化／推广关系的正式命题；


将“witness-cover classification”的新颖性限定为 Fibonacci exact-rank realization and lowered-label refinement of minimal multiplicative covers；


在摘要中避免给出“覆盖机制本身此前未知”的暗示。


在该修改完成前，我不能认可现行的全球优先权叙事。

2. 经修正后仍可成立的新颖性主张
在我所检索的文献中，未发现下列组合被先行工作明确给出：


Fibonacci 精确秩纤维 α−1(n) 的全部整除极小元与 lowered-label 原子覆盖之间的双射；


平方自由指标上，秩纯构造恰等于 Mn​ 的平方自由部分；


该切片按精确秩素数重数 a(nS​) 加权后的精确计数；


非平方自由梯级原子与平方自由切片之间的系统分界。


这里的否定性检索当然不是优先权证明；但在纳入 Wagner 后，我没有发现另一项会使上述 Fibonacci 专属结论直接退化为已知定理的文献。适宜的叙述应是“we are not aware of an earlier Fibonacci exact-fiber realization of this weighted multiplicative-cover structure”，而不是排他性的首次发现声明。

3. 素数逆向射线的优先权等级
我未在所核查的动力学论文中看到“无限素数逆向射线”被明确陈述。FitzGibbons 等人的正式问题确实仍停留在两两互素整数层面。Taylor & Francis Online+1
因此，该推论可以作为新结果保留。但其新颖性属于：

先前公开问题 + 经典精确秩素数存在定理之间此前未被写出的直接组合。

它不是新的 primitive-divisor theorem，也没有提供精确秩素数的新存在范围。建议将“解决公开问题”与“证明机制完全进口”同时写在定理附近，而不能只在后文附带说明。

4. 固定点分类、轨道终止性和 lcm 恒等式的署名必须改正
现稿把固定点分类和所有轨道终止性作为 FitzGibbons 等人的“recalled classification”使用。 这在二手引用意义上不算虚假，但不符合优先权审查所要求的第一来源署名：


z(n)=n 当且仅当 n=5k 或 12⋅5k 的证明应直接引用 Diego Marques。数学系


迭代最终到达固定点的较早证明应直接引用 Luca–Tron；其 Theorem 2.2 的证明明确以“the sequence of iterates eventually hits a fixed point”为起点。arXiv


α(lcm(a,b))=lcm(α(a),α(b)) 在 Luca–Tron 的基本引理中已明确列出；Renault 可以保留为更一般 (a,b)-Fibonacci 版本的来源，但不能替代对 Fibonacci 直接先行来源的署名。arXiv


**消除异议所需修改：**参考文献中补入 Marques 和 Luca–Tron，并在每一处区分：


original theorem；


later alternative proof；


present application。



5. Fibotomic 分解的历史归属不完整
现稿只把所用分解归于 Byer–Dvorachek–Eckard–Harrington–Wise–Wong。 其论文自身的历史说明是：Webb–Parberry 早在 1969 年研究了 Fibonacci polynomial 的不可约性，fibotomic polynomial 的定义由 Levy 于 2001 年引入，而 Byer 等人提供了较新的系统研究。NSF Public Access Repository
**消除异议所需修改：**补引 Levy 和 Webb–Parberry；准确说明本文究竟使用的是定义、分解恒等式、不可约性还是 Byer 等人的某个具体命题。仅以最新综述性来源覆盖历史来源，对本稿当前的优先权敏感程度而言不足。

6. Ck​∼bk​ 的引用链尚未闭合
稿件从 Hearne–Wagner 的精确式转向 Bender–Richmond–Wormald／Troyka 的 labelled split-graph 渐近，并直接写出
Ck​∼bk​∼ϑε​(⌊k/2⌋k​)2k2/4.

这里存在“标号对象”与“同构类对象”的引用风险：


Royle 明确说明其表格及对应关系计数的是非同构对象，而非 labelled objects。滑铁卢大学计算机科学系+1


Troyka 则明确区分 labelled 与 unlabeled split graphs，并把 Bender–Richmond–Wormald 的结果归入 labelled 枚举。Combinatorics+1


所声明的 Ck​∼bk​ 很可能是正确的，也可以从 Hearne–Wagner 精确式直接分析得到；问题在于现稿没有给出把“标号不可约覆盖”连接到“标号 split/bicolored graph”渐近的完整桥梁。
消除异议所需修改：


增加一个短引理，从 Hearne–Wagner 公式直接证明 Ck​/bk​→1；或


给出精确匹配 labelled minimal covers 的文献定理，而不依赖 Royle 的非标号对应；


若作者不愿补证明，则删除精确 ϑε​ 渐近，只保留并直接证明主结果实际需要的
logCk​=4log2​k2+O(k).



7. 对 Kiss、Stroiński、Sanna 和 Cera Da Conceição 的算术边界描述基本准确
现稿关于“固定整除条件不等于增长精确纤维”的区分是正确的：


Sanna 计数固定奇数 d 满足 d∣ρU​(p) 的素数，而非 ρU​(p)=d。arXiv


Cera Da Conceição 的 2026 年结果给出固定 d 下同一整除条件的 Dirichlet 密度，仍未局部化到增长的精确纤维。arXiv


Stroiński 的工作是关于在 Fibonacci 数处求值的 Dirichlet 乘积及原始因子求和，目前所列来源仍是 arXiv 预印本。arXiv


因此，Kiss／Stroiński 可以继续被称为最近的算术分布先行工作。需要删除的是把它们称为不加限定的“nearest prior work”；在结构方向上，Wagner 1978 明显更接近本文主分类。

三、是否足以发表于《The Fibonacci Quarterly》
现稿
不足。建议拒稿。
拒稿理由不是主题不合，也不是全部结果过弱，而是：


关键上界依赖未送审证明；


最近结构先行工作缺失，导致主定理的原创边界尚未正确建立；


若干基础进口定理未引用第一来源；


标号覆盖渐近的引用链不完整；


H1 与 BLMS 的冲突只陈述了一半；


素数逆向射线在摘要中的地位高于其证明机制所支持的地位。


这些问题无法通过局部勘误解决，故我不建议“major revision”后在同一轮直接接收。
完整重投后的期刊适配
《The Fibonacci Quarterly》明确以 Fibonacci 数及相关序列为核心，并自 2025 年起强调面向研究数学家和较早职业阶段读者的研究文章。本文的精确秩纤维、primitive divisors、fibotomic 分解及 Fibonacci 算术覆盖均高度符合其范围。Fibonacci Association
经实质修订后，以下组合足以支撑该刊论文：


lowered-label witness-cover classification；


与 Wagner minimal multiplicative covers 的严格比较；


平方自由精确纤维的规范识别；


加权精确计数；


一个完整、自足但不过度展开的熵上下界；


素数逆向射线作为简短应用。


我不会在这些修改完成后仅以“增量不足”为由拒绝。
若作者坚持保留当前全部长篇算术接口、条件猜想、支持谱及大量补编结构，而不愿压缩为更自足的 Fibonacci 主线，则 Journal of Integer Sequences 或 INTEGERS 在主题结构上也相称：前者专门刊载整数序列及密切相关主题，后者明确覆盖组合数论、乘法数论、序列、超图和极值组合。滑铁卢大学计算机科学系+1 但当前送审包在补足证明和优先权之前，不宜原样改投任何期刊。

四、重投前的必要修改
1. 提供完整、稳定、可审稿的证明包
必须将 prime-power lifting、prime–ladder dichotomy、梯级槽唯一性及全部被引用的补编结果纳入正式送审材料。正文中的每一项依赖均须指向有编号的命题。否则删除 Theorem 5.1、Theorem 5.10 精细上界及其后继结果。
这是接收的绝对前提。
2. 重写优先权小节
必须加入 Wagner 1978，并正式说明：


Wagner 条件；


素原子情形如何退化到 Wagner；


梯级原子如何构成真正推广；


本文的 Fibonacci 算术实现与加权部分何处新增。


这是第二项绝对前提。
3. 修正第一来源署名
至少补入：


Marques：固定点分类；


Luca–Tron：轨道终止性和 Fibonacci lcm 恒等式；


Levy、Webb–Parberry：fibotomic 历史；


Renault：保留为更一般序列版本；


FitzGibbons 等：仅用于其新问题、术语及其自身证明。


4. 降低素数逆向射线的标题权重
摘要中保留一至两句即可；改称 classical exact-rank existence 的直接应用。将“strictly strengthen and resolve”替换为“answer affirmatively in the stronger prime form”。不得暗示本文证明了新的 primitive-divisor existence theorem。
5. 补全 Ck​∼bk​ 的证明或删除精确渐近
必须解决 labelled/unlabeled 对象的转换。若只需二次对数主项，建议直接证明
logCk​=4log2​k2+O(k)
并把 theta-refined 精确式移至补编。
6. 改写 H1、H2
须明确 BLMS 猜想同时排斥 H1 和 H2。建议将两者改名为 sufficient maximum-window hypotheses；如仍称猜想，必须说明作者实际上在此点上采取了与 BLMS 不相容的预测。
7. 使 fibotomic 熵证明自足
增加关于 Fd​=Ψd​(1)、精确秩 radical 整除性、例外小秩和一致误差项的独立引理；同时补全历史引用。
8. 重组论文主线
建议顺序改为：


经典输入及优先权；


Wagner 比较与见证覆盖分类；


Fibonacci prime-power realization；


秩纯与平方自由切片；


加权覆盖与熵界；


素数逆向射线的短应用；


未解决的算术接口。


现行结构先突出直接进口推论，再进入真正原创的覆盖分类，不利于准确传达贡献层级。
9. 增加可核查实例
至少完整计算一个奇平方自由例、一个含例外支撑的偶数例，以及 169∈M91​∖R91rp​；每个实例应同时展示原子、Tn​、En​、覆盖和乘积。
10. 规范计算证据
若继续宣称“reproducible finite computations”，应提供稳定公开归档、环境锁定文件、SHA256 清单、运行入口以及“测试—定理”对应表。否则只应称为 finite checks，并删去可能被理解为独立证明证据的表述。有限核验不需要扩大范围；当前 n≤210 的覆盖已足以承担反例筛查功能。

最终裁决
**数学裁决：**没有发现见证覆盖主分类、平方自由规范切片、素数逆向射线或加权极值命题中的具体反例；但第 4、5 项主张的关键部分因补编缺失而尚不能认证。
**优先权裁决：**现行叙事不准确。遗漏 Wagner 1978 是实质性遗漏，足以阻止按现稿接受；固定点、轨道终止和 fibotomic 历史也须改为第一来源署名。对 Sanna、Cera、Stroiński 等增长精确秩分布边界的描述则基本准确。
**新颖性裁决：**素数逆向射线是新的直接推论，足以作为应用，但不足以作为独立核心定理。修正 Wagner 关系后，lowered-label Fibonacci witness classification、平方自由精确纤维识别及精确加权和仍构成可发表的新内容。
出版裁决：
Reject and invite a fully reconstructed resubmission.​
若完整补足证明、重建优先权边界并压缩叙事，我认为重投《The Fibonacci Quarterly》是合理且有实质成功可能的；不需要因结果层级本身而预先降投。

完整重投后的期刊适配
《The Fibonacci Quarterly》明确以 Fibonacci 数及相关序列为核心，并自 2025 年起强调面向研究数学家和较早职业阶段读者的研究文章。本文的精确秩纤维、primitive divisors、fibotomic 分解及 Fibonacci 算术覆盖均高度符合其范围。Fibonacci Association
经实质修订后，以下组合足以支撑该刊论文：


lowered-label witness-cover classification；


与 Wagner minimal multiplicative covers 的严格比较；


平方自由精确纤维的规范识别；


加权精确计数；


一个完整、自足但不过度展开的熵上下界；


素数逆向射线作为简短应用。


我不会在这些修改完成后仅以“增量不足”为由拒绝。
若作者坚持保留当前全部长篇算术接口、条件猜想、支持谱及大量补编结构，而不愿压缩为更自足的 Fibonacci 主线，则 Journal of Integer Sequences 或 INTEGERS 在主题结构上也相称：前者专门刊载整数序列及密切相关主题，后者明确覆盖组合数论、乘法数论、序列、超图和极值组合。滑铁卢大学计算机科学系+1 但当前送审包在补足证明和优先权之前，不宜原样改投任何期刊。

四、重投前的必要修改
1. 提供完整、稳定、可审稿的证明包
必须将 prime-power lifting、prime–ladder dichotomy、梯级槽唯一性及全部被引用的补编结果纳入正式送审材料。正文中的每一项依赖均须指向有编号的命题。否则删除 Theorem 5.1、Theorem 5.10 精细上界及其后继结果。
这是接收的绝对前提。
2. 重写优先权小节
必须加入 Wagner 1978，并正式说明：


Wagner 条件；


素原子情形如何退化到 Wagner；


梯级原子如何构成真正推广；


本文的 Fibonacci 算术实现与加权部分何处新增。


这是第二项绝对前提。
3. 修正第一来源署名
至少补入：


Marques：固定点分类；


Luca–Tron：轨道终止性和 Fibonacci lcm 恒等式；


Levy、Webb–Parberry：fibotomic 历史；


Renault：保留为更一般序列版本；


FitzGibbons 等：仅用于其新问题、术语及其自身证明。


4. 降低素数逆向射线的标题权重
摘要中保留一至两句即可；改称 classical exact-rank existence 的直接应用。将“strictly strengthen and resolve”替换为“answer affirmatively in the stronger prime form”。不得暗示本文证明了新的 primitive-divisor existence theorem。
5. 补全 Ck​∼bk​ 的证明或删除精确渐近
必须解决 labelled/unlabeled 对象的转换。若只需二次对数主项，建议直接证明
logCk​=4log2​k2+O(k)
并把 theta-refined 精确式移至补编。
6. 改写 H1、H2
须明确 BLMS 猜想同时排斥 H1 和 H2。建议将两者改名为 sufficient maximum-window hypotheses；如仍称猜想，必须说明作者实际上在此点上采取了与 BLMS 不相容的预测。
7. 使 fibotomic 熵证明自足
增加关于 Fd​=Ψd​(1)、精确秩 radical 整除性、例外小秩和一致误差项的独立引理；同时补全历史引用。
8. 重组论文主线
建议顺序改为：


经典输入及优先权；


Wagner 比较与见证覆盖分类；


Fibonacci prime-power realization；


秩纯与平方自由切片；


加权覆盖与熵界；


素数逆向射线的短应用；


未解决的算术接口。


现行结构先突出直接进口推论，再进入真正原创的覆盖分类，不利于准确传达贡献层级。
9. 增加可核查实例
至少完整计算一个奇平方自由例、一个含例外支撑的偶数例，以及 169∈M91​∖R91rp​；每个实例应同时展示原子、Tn​、En​、覆盖和乘积。
10. 规范计算证据
若继续宣称“reproducible finite computations”，应提供稳定公开归档、环境锁定文件、SHA256 清单、运行入口以及“测试—定理”对应表。否则只应称为 finite checks，并删去可能被理解为独立证明证据的表述。有限核验不需要扩大范围；当前 n≤210 的覆盖已足以承担反例筛查功能。

最终裁决
**数学裁决：**没有发现见证覆盖主分类、平方自由规范切片、素数逆向射线或加权极值命题中的具体反例；但第 4、5 项主张的关键部分因补编缺失而尚不能认证。
**优先权裁决：**现行叙事不准确。遗漏 Wagner 1978 是实质性遗漏，足以阻止按现稿接受；固定点、轨道终止和 fibotomic 历史也须改为第一来源署名。对 Sanna、Cera、Stroiński 等增长精确秩分布边界的描述则基本准确。
**新颖性裁决：**素数逆向射线是新的直接推论，足以作为应用，但不足以作为独立核心定理。修正 Wagner 关系后，lowered-label Fibonacci witness classification、平方自由精确纤维识别及精确加权和仍构成可发表的新内容。
出版裁决：
Reject and invite a fully reconstructed resubmission.​
若完整补足证明、重建优先权边界并压缩叙事，我认为重投《The Fibonacci Quarterly》是合理且有实质成功可能的；不需要因结果层级本身而预先降投。

种选择。

5. 秩窗去聚合与 fibotomic 熵界
5.1 秩窗去聚合
判断：推导在 prime–ladder 结论成立的前提下可信，但当前同样不完整。
窗口基数
#WJ​(n)=ℓ∈/J∏​νℓ​(n)
及
0≤logR(n)−logA∗(n)≤log2+ℓe∥n∑​loge
之后的均值估计没有明显错误。双重级数
p∑​j≥2∑​pjlogj​
绝对收敛，足以给出 O(xloglogx)。然而关键夹逼
P(n)≤R(n)≤P(n)+1
仍依赖未附的梯级原子分类。
**消除异议所需修改：**与上一项相同；缺少 prime–ladder 定理时，Theorem 5.1 不可保留为已证结果。
5.2 Fibotomic rank entropy
判断：证明思路成立，未发现反例；但文献归属和若干中间断言需写得更自足。
由 fibotomic 分解得到
Fd​=Ψd​(1)∈N,logFd​=φ(d)logφ+O(1),
精确秩素数的乘积整除 Fd​，再结合
p≡±1(modd)
对第 i 个精确秩素数给出下界，最后用 Stirling 公式得到系数 logφ/2。这一链条在数学上是一致的。
当前仍须补足：


明确证明带负 Möbius 指数的表达式为何等于正整数 Ψd​(1)；


单独陈述
α(p)=d∏​p∣Fd​
的引理，并说明不会因 Möbius 商式而发生约消；


将 d=3,5 以及空精确秩层 d=6,12 的处理统一列出；


在“一致 o(1)”处明确量词，而不是仅以二分情形隐含完成。


这些是可修复的写作缺口，不是我所发现的数学错误。

6. 固定总质量下的加权不可约覆盖极值
判断：命题及等号分类正确。
展开 wS​=1+xS​ 后，
S∈C∏​(1+xS​)≥1+S∈C∑​xS​
并对全部不可约覆盖求和。每个非空支撑至少出现一次，而全支撑只出现在单元素覆盖中，故得到锐界。对 k≥3，每个真非空支撑至少出现在两个不可约覆盖中，因此等号迫使全部真支撑的超额质量为零。
该结果是一个正确而有用的支持性引理，但证明十分初等，不宜作为论文独立主要创新。其价值在于精确说明“仅知 ω(Fn​) 的总质量不足以控制加权覆盖和”，而不是建立新的深层极值理论。

7. H1、H2 与 BLMS 猜想的关系尚未陈述完整
现稿只指出 BLMS 猜想会迫使 H2 失败。事实上，稿件自己的估计还推出 H1 也会失败。
在 BLMS 猜想下，对几乎所有平方自由合数 n，稿件已得到
logR(n)≥loglogn−ω(n)log2+O(1).
再利用平方自由整数上的正常阶
ω(n)=(1+o(1))loglogn,
可得
ω(n)logR(n)​≥1−log2+o(1)
在一个正密度集合上成立。因此，对任意
0<ε<1−log2,
H1 中的异常集不可能是 o(x)。 BLMS 原文确实猜测复合指标上 ω(Fn​)≫logn。Samir Siksek
这不使任何无条件定理失效，也不使“H1 蕴含条件正常阶”这一逻辑命题失效；但把 H1、H2 无限定地命名为“Conjecture”会隐去它们与一项已发表猜想的直接冲突。
消除异议所需修改：


最稳妥的方案是改称 sufficient hypotheses；


若作者坚持将 H1、H2 作为自己的反向猜想，则必须明确写出：BLMS 猜想将同时否定 H1 和 H2；


Corollary 5.14 应表述为纯条件推论，不应暗示这些条件目前有正面算术证据。



二、新颖性与优先权
1. Wagner（1978）是必须补入的最近结构先行工作
这是本报告最重要的优先权意见。
Wagner 的 Minimal multiplicative covers of an integer 并非仅仅讨论普通集合覆盖；其定义直接是由 n 的因子组成的元组，整体最小公倍数为 n，而删除任一分量后最小公倍数严格小于 n。他还明确指出，在 n 为不同素数乘积时，该问题推广了有限集的极小覆盖枚举。Mathematics+1
与本文的精确关系如下：


在平方自由极小元或一般秩纯素原子部分，d−(p)=1，本文见证条件正是 Wagner 条件的无序版本，再附加精确秩素数的算术选择；


对梯级原子，本文把“删除 di​”推广为“将 di​ 降到 di−​”；


本文的新增内容是这种降低操作的 Fibonacci prime-power 实现、唯一原子分解、平方自由规范切片以及精确秩乘数权重。


因此，现稿仍可能具有新意，但不能再将其表述为首次把精确纤维极小元归约为不可约 lcm-覆盖而不讨论 Wagner。
足以消除异议的修改：


在引言中增加 Wagner 1978 的独立小节；


给出上述退化／推广关系的正式命题；


将“witness-cover classification”的新颖性限定为 Fibonacci exact-rank realization and lowered-label refinement of minimal multiplicative covers；


在摘要中避免给出“覆盖机制本身此前未知”的暗示。


在该修改完成前，我不能认可现行的全球优先权叙事。

2. 经修正后仍可成立的新颖性主张
在我所检索的文献中，未发现下列组合被先行工作明确给出：


Fibonacci 精确秩纤维 α−1(n) 的全部整除极小元与 lowered-label 原子覆盖之间的双射；


平方自由指标上，秩纯构造恰等于 Mn​ 的平方自由部分；


该切片按精确秩素数重数 a(nS​) 加权后的精确计数；


非平方自由梯级原子与平方自由切片之间的系统分界。


这里的否定性检索当然不是优先权证明；但在纳入 Wagner 后，我没有发现另一项会使上述 Fibonacci 专属结论直接退化为已知定理的文献。适宜的叙述应是“we are not aware of an earlier Fibonacci exact-fiber realization of this weighted multiplicative-cover structure”，而不是排他性的首次发现声明。

3. 素数逆向射线的优先权等级
我未在所核查的动力学论文中看到“无限素数逆向射线”被明确陈述。FitzGibbons 等人的正式问题确实仍停留在两两互素整数层面。Taylor & Francis Online+1
因此，该推论可以作为新结果保留。但其新颖性属于：

先前公开问题 + 经典精确秩素数存在定理之间此前未被写出的直接组合。

它不是新的 primitive-divisor theorem，也没有提供精确秩素数的新存在范围。建议将“解决公开问题”与“证明机制完全进口”同时写在定理附近，而不能只在后文附带说明。

4. 固定点分类、轨道终止性和 lcm 恒等式的署名必须改正
现稿把固定点分类和所有轨道终止性作为 FitzGibbons 等人的“recalled classification”使用。 这在二手引用意义上不算虚假，但不符合优先权审查所要求的第一来源署名：


z(n)=n 当且仅当 n=5k 或 12⋅5k 的证明应直接引用 Diego Marques。数学系


迭代最终到达固定点的较早证明应直接引用 Luca–Tron；其 Theorem 2.2 的证明明确以“the sequence of iterates eventually hits a fixed point”为起点。arXiv


α(lcm(a,b))=lcm(α(a),α(b)) 在 Luca–Tron 的基本引理中已明确列出；Renault 可以保留为更一般 (a,b)-Fibonacci 版本的来源，但不能替代对 Fibonacci 直接先行来源的署名。arXiv


**消除异议所需修改：**参考文献中补入 Marques 和 Luca–Tron，并在每一处区分：


original theorem；


later alternative proof；


present application。



5. Fibotomic 分解的历史归属不完整
现稿只把所用分解归于 Byer–Dvorachek–Eckard–Harrington–Wise–Wong。 其论文自身的历史说明是：Webb–Parberry 早在 1969 年研究了 Fibonacci polynomial 的不可约性，fibotomic polynomial 的定义由 Levy 于 2001 年引入，而 Byer 等人提供了较新的系统研究。NSF Public Access Repository
**消除异议所需修改：**补引 Levy 和 Webb–Parberry；准确说明本文究竟使用的是定义、分解恒等式、不可约性还是 Byer 等人的某个具体命题。仅以最新综述性来源覆盖历史来源，对本稿当前的优先权敏感程度而言不足。

6. Ck​∼bk​ 的引用链尚未闭合
稿件从 Hearne–Wagner 的精确式转向 Bender–Richmond–Wormald／Troyka 的 labelled split-graph 渐近，并直接写出
Ck​∼bk​∼ϑε​(⌊k/2⌋k​)2k2/4.

这里存在“标号对象”与“同构类对象”的引用风险：


Royle 明确说明其表格及对应关系计数的是非同构对象，而非 labelled objects。滑铁卢大学计算机科学系+1


Troyka 则明确区分 labelled 与 unlabeled split graphs，并把 Bender–Richmond–Wormald 的结果归入 labelled 枚举。Combinatorics+1


所声明的 Ck​∼bk​ 很可能是正确的，也可以从 Hearne–Wagner 精确式直接分析得到；问题在于现稿没有给出把“标号不可约覆盖”连接到“标号 split/bicolored graph”渐近的完整桥梁。
消除异议所需修改：


增加一个短引理，从 Hearne–Wagner 公式直接证明 Ck​/bk​→1；或


给出精确匹配 labelled minimal covers 的文献定理，而不依赖 Royle 的非标号对应；


若作者不愿补证明，则删除精确 ϑε​ 渐近，只保留并直接证明主结果实际需要的
logCk​=4log2​k2+O(k).



7. 对 Kiss、Stroiński、Sanna 和 Cera Da Conceição 的算术边界描述基本准确
现稿关于“固定整除条件不等于增长精确纤维”的区分是正确的：


Sanna 计数固定奇数 d 满足 d∣ρU​(p) 的素数，而非 ρU​(p)=d。arXiv


Cera Da Conceição 的 2026 年结果给出固定 d 下同一整除条件的 Dirichlet 密度，仍未局部化到增长的精确纤维。arXiv


Stroiński 的工作是关于在 Fibonacci 数处求值的 Dirichlet 乘积及原始因子求和，目前所列来源仍是 arXiv 预印本。arXiv


因此，Kiss／Stroiński 可以继续被称为最近的算术分布先行工作。需要删除的是把它们称为不加限定的“nearest prior work”；在结构方向上，Wagner 1978 明显更接近本文主分类。

\section{A prime inverse-ray application}\label{sec:inverse-dynamics}
%% ====================================================================

Write $\operatorname{Fix}(\alpha)$ for the fixed points of the rank map.  For
$m\geq1$, define its \emph{fixed-point order} by
\[
 h(m):=\min\{k\geq0:\alpha^{\circ k}(m)\in
 \operatorname{Fix}(\alpha)\},
 \qquad
 \Lambda_k:=\{m\geq1:h(m)=k\}.
\]
For $x\in\operatorname{Fix}(\alpha)$, define its basin by
\[
 \Omega_x:=\{m\geq1:\alpha^{\circ j}(m)=x
 \text{ for some }j\geq0\}.
\]
These are the conventions of FitzGibbons--Javaheri--Miller--Verga
\cite{FitzGibbonsJavaheriMillerVerga2025Dynamics}. The fixed-point
classification is due to Marques \cite{Marques2012OrderAppearance}:
\[
 \operatorname{Fix}(\alpha)=\{5^a:a\geq0\}
 \cup\{12\cdot5^a:a\geq0\}
\]
Luca--Tron proved that every orbit reaches one of these fixed points
\cite{LucaTron2015SelfFibonacciDivisors}. In particular, the rank map has no
cycles of length greater than one. FitzGibbons--Javaheri--Miller--Verga are
cited below for their questions and terminology, not as the first source of
these two imported theorems.

No new prime-existence input is proved in this section. Every prime selected
below comes directly from Theorem~\ref{thm:primitive-divisors}.

Let $\varphi=(1+\sqrt5)/2$ and, for $t>0$, set
\[
 E_0(t)=t,\qquad E_{r+1}(t)=\varphi^{E_r(t)}\quad(r\geq0).
\]

\begin{theorem}[Prime inverse rays]\label{thm:prime-inverse-rays}
Let $y\geq7$ be an integer with $y\neq12$.  There are pairwise distinct
primes $p_1,p_2,\ldots$ such that, on putting $p_0=y$,
\[
 \alpha(p_r)=p_{r-1}\qquad(r\geq1).
\]
The primes can be chosen so that
\[
 p_r<E_r(y)\qquad(r\geq1).
\]
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Theorem~\ref{thm:primitive-divisors} gives an exact-rank prime $p_1$ with
$\alpha(p_1)=y$.  Since $y\geq7$, this prime is not $2$, $3$, or $5$, and
hence $p_1\geq7$.  Inductively, if $p_r\geq7$ is chosen, then
$p_r\notin\{6,12\}$, so the same theorem supplies a prime $p_{r+1}$ with
$\alpha(p_{r+1})=p_r$.  This constructs the ray indefinitely.

No $p_r$ equals $5$: for $r=1$ this follows from $\alpha(p_1)=y\neq5$, and
for $r>1$ the equality $p_r=5$ would propagate backwards under $\alpha$ to
$p_1=5$.  If two primes in the ray were equal, that repeated prime would be
periodic under $\alpha$.  The absence of cycles of length greater than one
would make it a fixed prime, whereas the fixed-point classification shows
that the only fixed prime is $5$.  Thus the $p_r$ are pairwise distinct.

Finally, $\alpha(p_r)=p_{r-1}$ implies $p_r\mid F_{p_{r-1}}$.  Since
$F_d<\varphi^d$ for $d\geq1$, we have
\[
 p_r\leq F_{p_{r-1}}<\varphi^{p_{r-1}}.
\]
Induction on $r$ now gives $p_r<E_r(y)$.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[Primes in every nontrivial fixed-point basin]
\label{cor:prime-basins}
For every fixed point $x>5$, the basin $\Omega_x$ contains infinitely many
primes.  More precisely, put
\[
 y_x=\begin{cases}7,&x=12,\\ x,&x\neq12.\end{cases}
\]
Then, for every $X>0$,
\[
 \#\{p\leq X:p\text{ prime},\ p\in\Omega_x\}
 \geq \max\bigl(\{r\geq1:E_r(y_x)\leq X\}\cup\{0\}\bigr).
\]
In particular, the lower bound is $\log^*X-O_x(1)$.
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
If $x\neq12$, then $x\geq7$ and Theorem~\ref{thm:prime-inverse-rays}
applied with $y=x$ gives distinct primes whose forward orbits reach $x$.
For the exceptional fixed point $12$, use
\[
 \alpha(7)=8,\qquad \alpha(8)=6,\qquad \alpha(6)=12
\]
and apply Theorem~\ref{thm:prime-inverse-rays} above $y=7$.  In either case,
the bound $p_r<E_r(y_x)$ gives the displayed counting estimate.  The
iterated-logarithm form follows by inverting the fixed-base exponential
recursion; changing the initial value changes the inverse height by
$O_x(1)$.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[Primes of every positive fixed-point order]
\label{cor:prime-fixed-order}
For every $k\geq1$, the class $\Lambda_k$ contains infinitely many primes.
More precisely, for every $X>0$,
\[
 \#\{p\leq X:p\text{ prime},\ h(p)=k\}
 \geq
 \#\{b\geq1:E_k(12\cdot5^b)\leq X\}.
\]
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
Fix $b\geq1$ and put $p_{0,b}=12\cdot5^b$.  Repeating the construction in
Theorem~\ref{thm:prime-inverse-rays} for $k$ steps gives primes
$p_{1,b},\ldots,p_{k,b}$ such that
\[
 \alpha(p_{j,b})=p_{j-1,b}\quad(1\leq j\leq k),
 \qquad p_{k,b}<E_k(12\cdot5^b).
\]
The points $p_{1,b},\ldots,p_{k,b}$ are not fixed, so
$h(p_{k,b})=k$.  If $b\neq b'$, then $p_{k,b}\neq p_{k,b'}$, since applying
$\alpha^{\circ k}$ would otherwise give
$12\cdot5^b=12\cdot5^{b'}$.  The asserted bound and infinitude follow.
\end{proof}

FitzGibbons--Javaheri--Miller--Verga asked whether every $\Lambda_k$ with
$k\geq1$ contains infinitely many relatively prime integers, and conjectured
that every $\Omega_x$ with fixed $x>5$ does so
\cite{FitzGibbonsJavaheriMillerVerga2025Dynamics}. As a direct corollary of
classical exact-rank prime existence, Corollaries~
\ref{cor:prime-fixed-order} and \ref{cor:prime-basins} answer both questions
affirmatively in the stronger prime form. The only input needed is the
classical exact-rank existence recorded in
Theorem~\ref{thm:primitive-divisors}; the witness-cover and rank-pure
machinery developed earlier is not used in their proofs.

 succeeded in 199ms:
\section{Support entropy and the unresolved arithmetic multiplicity}
\label{sec:growth}
%% ====================================================================

Write $\Omega(N)=\sum_p\nu_p(N)$ for the number of prime factors of $N$
counted with multiplicity. Set $R(2)=1$, consistently with
$\mathcal M_2=\varnothing$. For $n\ge3$ define the maximal atomic rank-window
multiplicity
\begin{equation}\label{eq:rank-window-multiplicity}
 R(n):=\max\!\left\{1,
 \max_{\varnothing\ne I\subseteq J\subseteq\mathcal P(n)}
       \#\mathcal A_{I,J}(n)\right\}.
\end{equation}
By the prime--ladder alternative, the nonsingleton families counted here are the
exact-rank windows $P_J(n)$, whereas every ladder family contributes at most
one atom. Thus $R(n)$ records the maximal atomic-family multiplicity retained
by the counting bounds below. It is an exact parameter for those bounds, but
it is not asserted to be an information-theoretically complete invariant of
the arithmetic outside the support combinatorics.

We first remove the contribution caused solely by collecting several exact
rank classes in one support window. For $d\mid n$, write
\[
 T_n(d):=\{\ell\in\mathcal P(n):
 \nu_\ell(d)=\nu_\ell(n)\}.
\]

\begin{theorem}[Mean rank-window deaggregation]
\label{thm:mean-rank-window-deaggregation}
Put
\[
 a(d):=\#\Pi_\alpha(d),\qquad
 \mathcal D^+(n):=\{d\mid n:T_n(d)\ne\varnothing\},
\]
and
\[
 A^*(n):=\max\left\{1,\max_{d\in\mathcal D^+(n)}a(d)\right\}.
\]
Then, as $x\to\infty$,
\begin{equation}\label{eq:mean-window-deaggregation}
 \sum_{3\le n\le x}\omega(n)\log R(n)
 =\sum_{3\le n\le x}\omega(n)\log A^*(n)
 +O(x\log\log x).
\end{equation}
Consequently the mean sparse-window hypothesis \textup{(H2)} is equivalent to
\begin{equation}\label{eq:visible-single-rank-interface}
 \sum_{3\le n\le x}\omega(n)
 \log\max\left\{1,
 \max_{\substack{d\mid n\\T_n(d)\ne\varnothing}}
 \#\Pi_\alpha(d)\right\}
 =o\bigl(x(\log\log x)^2\bigr).
\end{equation}
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
For nonempty $J\subseteq\mathcal P(n)$, let
\[
 W_J(n):=\{d\mid n:T_n(d)=J\}.
\]
If $n=\prod_\ell\ell^{e_\ell}$, then
\begin{equation}\label{eq:support-window-size}
 \#W_J(n)=\prod_{\ell\notin J}e_\ell.
\end{equation}
The prime atoms in the family with essential and full supports $(J,J)$ form
the finite disjoint union
\[
 \coprod_{d\in W_J(n)}\Pi_\alpha(d),
\]
because a prime has a unique rank of apparition. By the prime--ladder
alternative and the unique ladder-slot formula in
Lemma~\ref{thm:primitive-ladder-dichotomy} and
Proposition~\ref{prop:atomic-family-ladder-slots}, every remaining effective
atomic family is a ladder family with singleton essential support and at most
one atom. Therefore, with
\[
 P(n):=\max_{\varnothing\ne J\subseteq\mathcal P(n)}
       \sum_{d\in W_J(n)}a(d),
\]
one has
\begin{equation}\label{eq:prime-window-versus-R}
 P(n)\le R(n)\le P(n)+1.
\end{equation}
Every $d\in\mathcal D^+(n)$ belongs to one of the windows above. Hence
\[
 A^*(n)\le R(n)
 \le1+A^*(n)\max_J\#W_J(n)
 \le1+A^*(n)\prod_{\ell^{e_\ell}\parallel n}e_\ell.
\]
Writing
\[
 h(n):=\sum_{\ell^{e_\ell}\parallel n}\log e_\ell,
\]
and using $A^*(n)\ge1$ gives
\begin{equation}\label{eq:pointwise-window-deaggregation}
 0\le\log R(n)-\log A^*(n)\le\log2+h(n).
\end{equation}
For each exponent $e\ge2$, $\log e\le\sum_{2\le j\le e}\log j$, so
\[
 h(n)\le\sum_{\substack{p^j\mid n\\j\ge2}}\log j.
\]
The classical mean estimate \eqref{eq:mean-omega-input}, used uniformly with
$\log\log(3y)$ in place of $\log\log y$ for small $y$, yields
\begin{align*}
 \sum_{n\le x}\omega(n)h(n)
 &\le\sum_{\substack{p^j\le x\\j\ge2}}\log j
       \sum_{m\le x/p^j}\omega(p^jm)\\
 &\le\sum_{\substack{p^j\le x\\j\ge2}}\log j
       \sum_{m\le x/p^j}(1+\omega(m))\\
 &\ll x\log\log x\sum_p\sum_{j\ge2}\frac{\log j}{p^j}
 \ll x\log\log x.
\end{align*}
The double series converges absolutely. Summing
\eqref{eq:pointwise-window-deaggregation} and using
$\sum_{n\le x}\omega(n)\ll x\log\log x$ proves
\eqref{eq:mean-window-deaggregation}; the stated equivalence follows because
$x\log\log x=o(x(\log\log x)^2)$.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[Single-rank sufficient and necessary interfaces]
\label{cor:single-rank-mean-interfaces}
A sufficient condition for \textup{(H2)} is
\begin{equation}\label{eq:single-rank-sufficient-condition}
 \sum_{d\le x}
 \frac{(\omega(d)+\log\log x)\log\max\{1,a(d)\}}{d}
 =o\bigl((\log\log x)^2\bigr).
\end{equation}
Conversely, \textup{(H2)} implies the diagonal necessary condition
\begin{equation}\label{eq:single-rank-necessary-condition}
 \sum_{n\le x}\omega(n)\log\max\{1,a(n)\}
 =o\bigl(x(\log\log x)^2\bigr).
\end{equation}
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
Since
\[
 \log A^*(n)\le\sum_{d\mid n}\log\max\{1,a(d)\},
\]
the substitution $n=dm$ and $\omega(dm)\le\omega(d)+\omega(m)$ gives
\[
 \sum_{n\le x}\omega(n)\log A^*(n)
 \ll x\sum_{d\le x}
 \frac{(\omega(d)+\log\log x)\log\max\{1,a(d)\}}{d}.
\]
Thus \eqref{eq:single-rank-sufficient-condition} and
Theorem~\ref{thm:mean-rank-window-deaggregation} imply \textup{(H2)}. For the
necessary condition, $n\in\mathcal D^+(n)$, and hence
$R(n)\ge A^*(n)\ge\max\{1,a(n)\}$; summation proves the claim.
\end{proof}

The Fibonacci polynomials were studied by Webb--Parberry
\cite{WebbParberry1969FibonacciPolynomials}; Levy introduced the fibotomic
polynomials and proved their irreducibility over $\mathbb Q$
\cite{Levy2001FibonacciPolynomials}; and Byer--Dvorachek--Eckard--Harrington--
Wise--Wong developed their later systematic theory
\cite{CameronCountsLundMillerPiechnikWong2022Fibotomic}. We use only the
definition and factorization identity recorded in the following lemma.

\begin{lemma}[Fibotomic integer and exact-rank radical]
\label{lem:fibotomic-integer-radical}
Let $d\ge3$, let $\Psi_d(x)$ be the $d$th fibotomic polynomial, and put
\[
 \mathfrak F_d:=\prod_{r\mid d}F_r^{\mu(d/r)}.
\]
Then
\begin{equation}\label{eq:fibotomic-integer-identity}
 \mathfrak F_d=\Psi_d(1)\in\mathbb N,
\end{equation}
and
\begin{equation}\label{eq:exact-rank-radical-divides-fibotomic}
 \prod_{p\in\Pi_\alpha(d)}p\mid\mathfrak F_d.
\end{equation}
For $d=3,5$ the exact-rank radicals are respectively $2$ and $5$; for
$d=6,12$ the exact-rank layer is empty, so the product in the second display
is $1$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
The polynomial factorization
\[
 F_d(x)=\prod_{r\mid d}\Psi_r(x)
\]
and M\"obius inversion in the multiplicative group of nonzero rational
functions give
\[
 \Psi_d(x)=\prod_{r\mid d}F_r(x)^{\mu(d/r)}.
\]
Evaluation at $x=1$ proves
$\mathfrak F_d=\Psi_d(1)$. The fibotomic polynomial has positive integral
value at $1$, proving \eqref{eq:fibotomic-integer-identity} despite the
negative exponents in the M\"obius expression.

If $p\in\Pi_\alpha(d)$, then $p\mid F_d$ but $p\nmid F_r$ for every proper
divisor $r$ of $d$. In the integral factorization
$F_d=\prod_{r\mid d}\Psi_r(1)$, any divisibility
$p\mid\Psi_r(1)$ with $r<d$ would imply $p\mid F_r$, because
$\Psi_r(1)\mid F_r$. Hence $p\mid\Psi_d(1)=\mathfrak F_d$. Distinct
exact-rank primes are coprime, which proves the radical divisibility without
any appeal to cancellation in the M\"obius quotient. The small-rank claims
follow from $\alpha(2)=3$, $\alpha(5)=5$, and the absence of exact-rank
primes at ranks $6$ and $12$.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Fibotomic rank entropy]
\label{thm:fibotomic-rank-entropy}
Let $\varphi=(1+\sqrt5)/2$, let $\phi_{\rm E}$ denote Euler's totient
function, and, for $d\geq3$, put
\[
 \mathfrak F_d:=\prod_{r\mid d}F_r^{\mu(d/r)}.
\]
For $k\geq0$, set $m=\lfloor k/2\rfloor$ and
\[
 \mathcal H_d(k):=
 k\log\frac{2d}{3}+2\log(m!)+(k-2m)\log(m+1).
\]
There is an absolute constant $C_F$ such that, for every $d\geq3$,
\begin{equation}\label{eq:fibotomic-rank-entropy}
 \mathcal H_d(a(d))\leq \phi_{\rm E}(d)\log\varphi+C_F.
\end{equation}
More generally, every finitely supported family of nonnegative real numbers
$(\lambda_d)_{d\geq3}$ satisfies
\begin{equation}\label{eq:weighted-fibotomic-rank-entropy}
 \sum_{d\geq3}\lambda_d\mathcal H_d(a(d))
 \leq (\log\varphi)\sum_{d\geq3}\lambda_d\phi_{\rm E}(d)
       +C_F\sum_{d\geq3}\lambda_d.
\end{equation}
Uniformly as $d\to\infty$,
\begin{equation}\label{eq:pointwise-exact-rank-bound}
 a(d)\leq
 \left(\frac{\log\varphi}{2}+o(1)\right)
 \frac{\phi_{\rm E}(d)}{\log d}.
\end{equation}
Consequently, uniformly as $n\to\infty$,
\begin{equation}\label{eq:visible-exact-rank-bound}
 A^*(n)\leq
 \left(\frac{\log\varphi}{2}+o(1)\right)
 \frac{\phi_{\rm E}(n)}{\log n}.
\end{equation}
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Binet's formula, in the form
\[
 F_r=\frac{\varphi^r}{\sqrt5}
 \bigl(1-(-\varphi^{-2})^r\bigr),
\]
and the identities
$\sum_{r\mid d}\mu(d/r)=0$ and
$\sum_{r\mid d}r\mu(d/r)=\phi_{\rm E}(d)$ show that
\begin{equation}\label{eq:fibotomic-binet-error}
 \log\mathfrak F_d=\phi_{\rm E}(d)\log\varphi+E_d,
 \qquad
 |E_d|\leq
 C_F:=\sum_{j\geq1}
 \left|\log\bigl(1-(-\varphi^{-2})^j\bigr)\right|.
\end{equation}
The series defining $C_F$ converges geometrically.

Lemma~\ref{lem:fibotomic-integer-radical} gives the exact-rank radical
divisibility with no cancellation ambiguity.
For $p\notin\{2,5\}$, the classical rank congruence gives
\[
 d=\alpha(p)\mid p-\left(\frac5p\right),
\]
see \cite{Wall1960FibonacciModm,Vinson1963RankApparition}. Thus
$p\equiv\pm1\pmod d$. The ranks $d=3,5$ have respectively the single
exact-rank primes $2,5$ and satisfy the estimate directly; the layers
$d=6,12$ are empty and also satisfy it. For every remaining $d$, write the
exact-rank primes in increasing order as
$p_1<\cdots<p_k$. There are at most two candidates in each pair of residue
classes $jd\pm1$, whence
\[
 p_i\geq d\left\lceil\frac i2\right\rceil-1
 \geq\frac{2d}{3}\left\lceil\frac i2\right\rceil.
\]
It follows that, with $m=\lfloor k/2\rfloor$,
\begin{equation}\label{eq:rank-congruence-product-bound}
 \prod_{i=1}^kp_i\geq
 \left(\frac{2d}{3}\right)^k(m!)^2(m+1)^{k-2m}.
\end{equation}
Combining \eqref{eq:fibotomic-binet-error}--
\eqref{eq:rank-congruence-product-bound} and taking logarithms proves
\eqref{eq:fibotomic-rank-entropy}. Multiplication by $\lambda_d\geq0$ and
finite summation proves \eqref{eq:weighted-fibotomic-rank-entropy}.

Stirling's lower bound applied in \eqref{eq:fibotomic-rank-entropy} gives,
with an absolute implied constant,
\begin{equation}\label{eq:rank-entropy-coarse}
 a(d)\bigl(\log d+\log\max\{1,a(d)\}-O(1)\bigr)
 \leq \phi_{\rm E}(d)\log\varphi+O(1).
\end{equation}
Fix $\varepsilon>0$. If $a(d)\leq\phi_{\rm E}(d)/(\log d)^2$, then
\eqref{eq:pointwise-exact-rank-bound} follows immediately. Otherwise the
standard uniform lower bound
$\phi_{\rm E}(d)\gg d/\log\log(3d)$
\cite{HardyWright2008Introduction} implies
\[
 \log a(d)=\log d+O(\log\log d)=\log d+o(\log d).
\]
Substitution in \eqref{eq:rank-entropy-coarse} yields, for all sufficiently
large $d$ depending only on $\varepsilon$,
$(2+o(1))a(d)\log d\leq\phi_{\rm E}(d)\log\varphi$, proving
\eqref{eq:pointwise-exact-rank-bound} with its $o(1)$ uniform over every
$d$ beyond that threshold.

For \eqref{eq:visible-exact-rank-bound}, the case $A^*(n)=1$ follows from
$\phi_{\rm E}(n)/\log n\to\infty$. Otherwise choose
$d_n\in\mathcal D^+(n)$ with $A^*(n)=a(d_n)$. The preceding estimate has
the coarse consequence $a(d)\ll d/\log(2d)$. If
$d_n\leq n/(\log n)^3$, the lower bound for $\phi_{\rm E}(n)$ gives
\[
 a(d_n)=o\left(\frac{\phi_{\rm E}(n)}{\log n}\right).
\]
If $d_n>n/(\log n)^3$, then
$\log d_n=(1+o(1))\log n$ and, since $d_n\mid n$,
$\phi_{\rm E}(d_n)\leq\phi_{\rm E}(n)$. Applying
\eqref{eq:pointwise-exact-rank-bound} at $d_n$ completes the proof. The two
cases use thresholds depending only on $\varepsilon$, so the final $o(1)$ is
uniform over all $n$ beyond a common threshold.
\end{proof}



 succeeded in 210ms:
\section{Rank-pure covers and the canonical squarefree slice}
\label{sec:rank-pure}

For a finite set $V$, write $\operatorname{MinCov}(V)$ for the set of
irredundant covers of $V$ by distinct nonempty subsets: a family
$\mathcal C$ belongs to $\operatorname{MinCov}(V)$ when
$\bigcup_{S\in\mathcal C}S=V$ and every $S\in\mathcal C$ contains an element
outside $\bigcup_{T\in\mathcal C\setminus\{S\}}T$.

\begin{theorem}[Rank-pure universality]\label{thm:rank-pure-universality}
Let $n\ge3$, put $V=\mathcal P(n)$, and, for every nonempty $S\subseteq V$,
define
\[
 n_S:=\prod_{\ell\in S}\ell^{\nu_\ell(n)},\qquad
 a_n(S):=\#\Pi_\alpha(n_S),
\]
and
\[
 \mathcal E_n:=\{S\subseteq V:S\ne\varnothing,\ n_S\in\{2,6,12\}\}.
\]
Then $\#\mathcal E_n\le2$, and every member of $\mathcal E_n$ has cardinality
at most two. Define the rank-pure prime sector
\[
 \mathcal R_n^{\rm rp}:=
 \left\{
 \prod_{S\in\mathcal C}p_S:
 \begin{array}{l}
  \mathcal C\in\operatorname{MinCov}(V),\quad
  \mathcal C\cap\mathcal E_n=\varnothing,\\
  p_S\in\Pi_\alpha(n_S)\quad(S\in\mathcal C)
 \end{array}
 \right\}.
\]
Then $\mathcal R_n^{\rm rp}\subseteq\mathcal M_n$, the displayed representation is
unique, and
\begin{equation}\label{eq:rank-pure-weighted-sector}
 \#\mathcal R_n^{\rm rp}=
 \sum_{\substack{\mathcal C\in\operatorname{MinCov}(V)\\
                   \mathcal C\cap\mathcal E_n=\varnothing}}
 \prod_{S\in\mathcal C}a_n(S),
 \qquad
 a_n(S)=\sum_{d\mid n_S}\mu(n_S/d)\,\omega(F_d).
\end{equation}
Moreover, the support hypergraph of the product attached to $\mathcal C$ has
edge set exactly $\mathcal C$. In particular, connected minimal covers map to
$\mathcal M_n^{\rm conn}$, with their complete hypergraph structure preserved.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
The map $S\mapsto n_S$ is injective. If $n_S=2$, then
$S=\{2\}$ and $\nu_2(n)=1$. If $n_S=6$, then
$S=\{2,3\}$ and $\nu_2(n)=\nu_3(n)=1$; if $n_S=12$, then
$S=\{2,3\}$, $\nu_2(n)=2$, and $\nu_3(n)=1$. Thus the ranks $6$ and $12$
cannot both occur, while rank $2$ can coexist only with rank $6$. This proves
the assertions about $\mathcal E_n$.

Fix $\mathcal C\in\operatorname{MinCov}(V)$ avoiding $\mathcal E_n$. For
every $S\in\mathcal C$, the integer $n_S$ is at least $3$ and is not $6$ or
$12$. Theorem~\ref{thm:primitive-divisors} therefore gives a prime
$p_S\in\Pi_\alpha(n_S)$. Distinct supports have distinct ranks, so the primes
$p_S$ are distinct. Since $p_S$ is a prime atom and $d^-(p_S)=1$,
\[
 E_n(p_S)=T_n(p_S)=S.
\]
Consequently the coverage and private-coordinate conditions defining
$\operatorname{MinCov}(V)$ are exactly the witness-cover conditions for
$\{p_S:S\in\mathcal C\}$. Theorem~\ref{thm:witness-classification} places
$\prod_{S\in\mathcal C}p_S$ in $\mathcal M_n$.

Unique factorization recovers every selected prime from the product; its rank
$\alpha(p_S)=n_S$ then recovers $S$. Hence both the cover and all prime choices
are recovered, proving injectivity and the weighted count in
\eqref{eq:rank-pure-weighted-sector}. The formula for $a_n(S)$ is
Lemma~\ref{lem:primitive-prime-mobius-count}. Finally,
\[
 \operatorname{supp}_n(\alpha(p_S))=
 \operatorname{supp}_n(n_S)=S,
\]
so Definition~\ref{def:support-hypergraph} gives precisely the edge set
$\mathcal C$ and therefore preserves connected components.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[Canonical minimal-transversal model]
\label{cor:rank-pure-minimal-transversals}
Retain the notation of Theorem~\ref{thm:rank-pure-universality}. Define a
hypergraph $\mathcal H_n^{\rm rp}$ with vertex set
\[
 U_n:=\{(S,p):\varnothing\ne S\subseteq V,\ S\notin\mathcal E_n,
                    \ p\in\Pi_\alpha(n_S)\}
\]
and, for each $\ell\in V$, the coordinate edge
\[
 X_\ell:=\{(S,p)\in U_n:\ell\in S\}.
\]
Multiplication induces a bijection
\begin{equation}\label{eq:rank-pure-minimal-transversal-bijection}
 \operatorname{MinTr}(\mathcal H_n^{\rm rp})
 \longrightarrow \mathcal R_n^{\rm rp},
 \qquad Q\longmapsto\prod_{(S,p)\in Q}p,
\end{equation}
where $\operatorname{MinTr}$ denotes the inclusion-minimal transversals.
Hence the weighted cover polynomial
\eqref{eq:rank-pure-weighted-sector} is exactly a minimal-transversal count of
a canonically defined hypergraph. By the standard split-incidence
correspondence \cite[Proposition~30]{KanteLimouzyMaryNourine2014DominatingSets},
it also counts the minimal connected dominating sets of the associated split
graph.
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
A set $Q\subseteq U_n$ is a transversal precisely when its support labels
cover $V$. If a minimal transversal contained $(S,p)$ and $(S,q)$ with
$p\ne q$, removing either vertex would preserve every coordinate edge hit by
it, a contradiction. Thus its support labels are distinct. Minimality also
says that every $(S,p)\in Q$ has a coordinate $\ell\in S$ belonging to no
other selected support. The support labels therefore form an irredundant
cover. Conversely, selected prime choices over an irredundant cover form a
minimal transversal, since every selected vertex has such a private
coordinate edge. Theorem~\ref{thm:rank-pure-universality} and unique
factorization now prove that multiplication is the asserted bijection.
\end{proof}

The rank-pure sector has a canonical description on squarefree indices.  In
particular, it is not then an auxiliary class defined only through the cover
construction.

\begin{corollary}[Canonical squarefree exact-fiber slice]
\label{cor:canonical-squarefree-slice}
Let $n\ge3$ be squarefree.  Then
\begin{equation}\label{eq:canonical-squarefree-slice}
 \mathcal R_n^{\rm rp}
 =\mathcal M_n\cap\{q:\mu^2(q)=1\}
 =\min_{\mid}\{q:\alpha(q)=n,\ \mu^2(q)=1\}.
\end{equation}
Thus the weighted sum in
\eqref{eq:rank-pure-weighted-sector} counts exactly the divisibility-minimal
squarefree elements of the standard exact fiber $\alpha^{-1}(n)$.
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
Proposition~\ref{prop:rank-basics} gives
$B_n=\{q:\alpha(q)=n\}=\alpha^{-1}(n)$.  If a squarefree member of $B_n$
has a proper divisor in $B_n$, that divisor is again squarefree.  Hence
minimality in the squarefree part of the exact fiber is equivalent to
membership in $\mathcal M_n$.

Every element of $\mathcal R_n^{\rm rp}$ is a product of distinct primes and
belongs to $\mathcal M_n$, which proves one inclusion in the first equality.
Conversely, let $m\in\mathcal M_n$ be squarefree.  Its unique atomic
factorization from Theorem~\ref{thm:witness-classification} consists only of
prime atoms $p$.  Put $S=T_n(p)$.  Since $\alpha(p)\mid n$ and $n$ is
squarefree,
\[
 \alpha(p)=\prod_{\ell\in S}\ell=n_S.
\]
Moreover $d^-(p)=1$, so $E_n(p)=T_n(p)=S$.  The witness-cover conditions are
therefore precisely that the distinct supports $S$ form an irredundant cover
of $\mathcal P(n)$.  No such support lies in $\mathcal E_n$, because the
corresponding prime $p$ itself has exact rank $n_S$.  Thus the atomic
factorization of $m$ is one of the uniquely represented products defining
$\mathcal R_n^{\rm rp}$.  This proves the reverse inclusion and the result.
\end{proof}

\subsection*{Worked exact-fiber layers}

\begin{example}[An odd squarefree layer without exceptions]
Let $n=15$ and label its prime coordinates by $V=\{3,5\}$. The relevant
Fibonacci factorizations are
\[
 F_3=2,\qquad F_5=5,\qquad F_{15}=2\cdot5\cdot61,
\]
so
\[
 \Pi_\alpha(3)=\{2\},\qquad
 \Pi_\alpha(5)=\{5\},\qquad
 \Pi_\alpha(15)=\{61\}.
\]
There is no exceptional support. For the prime atoms one has
\[
 T_{15}(2)=E_{15}(2)=\{3\},\quad
 T_{15}(5)=E_{15}(5)=\{5\},\quad
 T_{15}(61)=E_{15}(61)=\{3,5\}.
\]
The two irredundant covers of $V$ are
$\{\{3,5\}\}$ and $\{\{3\},\{5\}\}$. They give the products
\[
 61\qquad\text{and}\qquad2\cdot5=10,
\]
respectively. Thus the canonical squarefree slice is
$\mathcal R_{15}^{\rm rp}=\{10,61\}$, with each atom, support, essential
support, cover, and product visible in the displays.
\end{example}

\begin{example}[An even layer with exceptional supports]
Let $n=6$ and $V=\{2,3\}$. Here the supports $\{2\}$ and $\{2,3\}$ carry
the exceptional ranks $2$ and $6$, respectively. The only nonexceptional
support $\{3\}$ cannot cover $V$, so $\mathcal R_6^{\rm rp}=\varnothing$.
On the other hand, $4$ is a ladder atom with
\[
 d(4)=\alpha(4)=6,\qquad d^-(4)=\alpha(2)=3,
\]
and hence
\[
 T_6(4)=\{2,3\},\qquad E_6(4)=\{2\}.
\]
The singleton $\{4\}$ is a witness cover and its product is $4$. Directly,
$F_6=8$ and the ranks of its divisors $1,2,4,8$ are $1,3,6,6$, so
$\mathcal M_6=\{4\}$. This shows exactly how the exceptional support removes
the prime realization while a lowered-label ladder remains.
\end{example}

\begin{example}[The ladder boundary at $n=91$]
The coordinates are $V=\{7,13\}$. Since $F_7=13$, one has
$\alpha(13)=7$ and $\nu_{13}(F_7)=1$. Proposition~
\ref{prop:prime-power-ladders} therefore gives
\[
 d(13^2)=\alpha(13^2)=13\alpha(13)=91,
 \qquad d^-(13^2)=7.
\]
Consequently
\[
 T_{91}(169)=\{7,13\},\qquad E_{91}(169)=\{13\}.
\]
The singleton cover $\{169\}$ is irredundant, and its product is $169$, so
$169\in\mathcal M_{91}$. It is a squareful ladder atom and therefore lies
outside the prime-only rank-pure sector:
\[
 169\in\mathcal M_{91}\setminus\mathcal R_{91}^{\rm rp}.
\]
\end{example}

\begin{corollary}[Odd-layer cover universality]
\label{cor:odd-rank-pure-universality}
Let $n\ge3$ be odd and put $k=\omega(n)$. Then $\mathcal E_n=\varnothing$.
After fixing one prime $\pi_S\in\Pi_\alpha(n_S)$ for every nonempty
$S\subseteq\mathcal P(n)$, the map
\[
 \mathcal C\longmapsto\prod_{S\in\mathcal C}\pi_S
\]
embeds $\operatorname{MinCov}(\mathcal P(n))$ into $\mathcal M_n$ and
preserves the support hypergraph. Hence, if $C_k$ denotes the number of minimal
covers of a labelled $k$-set, then
\begin{equation}\label{eq:odd-minimal-cover-bound}
 \#\mathcal M_n\ge C_k,
 \qquad
 \widthop(B_n)\ge C_k.
\end{equation}
Through Royle's correspondence
\cite{Royle2000SetCoversSplitGraphs}, every isomorphism type of split graph on
$k$ vertices consequently has a rank-pure Fibonacci realization on the prime
coordinates of $n$. This last sentence concerns unlabelled isomorphism types
only and is not used to enumerate the labelled covers below.
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
Every nonempty $n_S$ is odd and at least $3$, so no support is exceptional.
Theorem~\ref{thm:rank-pure-universality} gives the embedding and support
preservation. Minimal generators form an antichain, which proves the width
bound. Royle's theorem is a correspondence between isomorphism types of split
graphs and minimal covers; choosing a labelling of a representative cover and
then applying the embedding gives the final assertion.
\end{proof}

We next fix the classical enumeration input. Let $C_{k,s}$ count the minimal
covers of a labelled $k$-set having
$s$ members, put $C_k=\sum_s C_{k,s}$, and put
\[
 b_k:=\sum_{s=0}^k\binom{k}{s}2^{s(k-s)}.
\]
Hearne and Wagner \cite{HearneWagner1973MinimalCovers} proved
\begin{equation}\label{eq:hearne-wagner}
 C_{k,s}=\sum_{j=s}^k\binom{k}{j}
 \genfrac{\{}{\}}{0pt}{}{j}{s}
 (2^s-s-1)^{k-j}.
\end{equation}
The prime coordinates of $n$ are specific primes, so the arithmetic embedding
uses these labelled covers: there is no quotient by $S_k$ and no division by
$k!$. Royle's correspondence above concerns isomorphism classes and supplies
only the realization statement. The labelled central sum $b_k$ is the object
in the split-graph enumeration of
Bender--Richmond--Wormald \cite{BenderRichmondWormald1985ChordalSplit}, in the
form recorded by Troyka \cite[Section~3]{Troyka2019SplitGraphs}. The following
direct comparison closes the conversion from the Hearne--Wagner labelled
formula to that labelled central sum.

\begin{lemma}[Labelled minimal-cover comparison]
\label{lem:labelled-minimal-cover-comparison}
As $k\to\infty$,
\[
 \frac{C_k}{b_k}\longrightarrow1.
\]
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
Fix an order on the labelled ground set. In a minimal cover with $s$ members,
select the least private element of each member. The selected elements form an
$s$-set $P$; no selected element can occur in another member, and each member
is recovered from its selected element and a subset of the remaining
$k-s$ elements. This canonical encoding is injective, so
\[
 C_{k,s}\le\binom{k}{s}2^{s(k-s)}.
\]
The $j=s$ term of \eqref{eq:hearne-wagner} gives the matching lower bound
\[
 C_{k,s}\ge\binom{k}{s}(2^s-s-1)^{k-s}.
\]
Uniformly for $k/3\le s\le2k/3$,
\[
 \left(1-\frac{s+1}{2^s}\right)^{k-s}=1-o(1).
\]
The contribution to $b_k$ from the complementary values of $s$ is at most
\[
 2^k2^{2k^2/9}=o\bigl(2^{\lfloor k^2/4\rfloor}\bigr)=o(b_k).
\]
Summing the two bounds over the central range therefore gives
$(1-o(1))b_k\le C_k\le b_k$, which proves the claim.
\end{proof}

Consequently, with
\[
 \vartheta_0:=\sum_{r\in\mathbb Z}2^{-r^2},\qquad
 \vartheta_1:=\sum_{r\in\mathbb Z}2^{-(r+1/2)^2},\qquad
 \varepsilon\equiv k\pmod2,
\]
the published labelled asymptotic for $b_k$ and
Lemma~\ref{lem:labelled-minimal-cover-comparison} give
\begin{equation}\label{eq:minimal-cover-theta}
 C_k\sim b_k\sim
 \vartheta_\varepsilon\binom{k}{\lfloor k/2\rfloor}2^{k^2/4}.
\end{equation}
In particular, $b_{k-1}/b_k=O(2^{-k/2})$.

\begin{corollary}[Weighted-cover obstruction on odd layers]
\label{cor:odd-weighted-cover-obstruction}
Let $n\ge3$ be odd, put $V=\mathcal P(n)$ and $k=\omega(n)$, and retain the
weights $a_n(S)=\#\Pi_\alpha(n_S)$ from
Theorem~\ref{thm:rank-pure-universality}. Then
\begin{equation}\label{eq:odd-weighted-rank-pure-sector}
 \#\mathcal R_n^{\rm rp}
 =\sum_{\mathcal C\in\operatorname{MinCov}(V)}
       \prod_{S\in\mathcal C}a_n(S)
 \le\#\mathcal M_n.
\end{equation}
Every weight in this sum is positive. Consequently, with
\begin{equation}\label{eq:weighted-cover-excess}
 \mathcal W(n):=\frac1{C_k}
 \sum_{\mathcal C\in\operatorname{MinCov}(V)}
       \prod_{S\in\mathcal C}a_n(S)\ge1,
\end{equation}
one has
\begin{equation}\label{eq:odd-rank-pure-log-decomposition}
 \log\#\mathcal R_n^{\rm rp}
 =\frac{\log2}{4}k^2+\log\mathcal W(n)+O(k).
\end{equation}
Therefore an almost-all asymptotic
\[
 \log\#\mathcal M_n\sim\frac{\log2}{4}(\log\log n)^2
\]
would necessarily imply
\begin{equation}\label{eq:weighted-cover-dispersion-interface}
 \log\mathcal W(n)=o\bigl((\log\log n)^2\bigr)
\end{equation}
outside an $o(x)$ exceptional set of odd $n\le x$.
The converse is not asserted: ladder atoms and other non-rank-pure witness
covers still require an upper bound.  This distinction occurs already at the
odd squarefree index $91$, where $\alpha(13)=7$ and
$\alpha(13^2)=91$, so $169\in\mathcal M_{91}\setminus\mathcal R_{91}^{\rm rp}$.
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
For odd $n$, every $n_S$ is odd and at least $3$, so
$\mathcal E_n=\varnothing$ and the exact-rank existence theorem gives
$a_n(S)\ge1$.  The weighted identity and inclusion are
\eqref{eq:rank-pure-weighted-sector}.  The asymptotic
\eqref{eq:minimal-cover-theta} gives
$\log C_k=(\log2)k^2/4+O(k)$ and proves
\eqref{eq:odd-rank-pure-log-decomposition}.

If the displayed almost-all formula for $\mathcal M_n$ held, then it would
hold outside an $o(x)$ subset of the odd integers.  Intersecting with the
Hardy--Ramanujan normal-order set and using
$\#\mathcal R_n^{\rm rp}\le\#\mathcal M_n$ forces
\eqref{eq:weighted-cover-dispersion-interface}.  Finally, the classical
lifting formula gives $\alpha(13^2)=13\alpha(13)=91$ and
$\alpha(13)=7<91$.  Thus $13^2$ is an atom of rank $91$ and the singleton
witness cover $\{13^2\}$ gives the stated strict inclusion.
\end{proof}

The following sharp extremal calculation separates total primitive-prime
mass from the dispersion information retained by the cover polynomial.

\begin{proposition}[Sharp cumulative-data obstruction]
\label{prop:sharp-cumulative-data-obstruction}
Let $V$ be a labelled $k$-set, $k\ge1$, and assign a real weight
$w_S\ge1$ to every nonempty $S\subseteq V$.  Put
\[
 Z_V(w):=\sum_{\mathcal C\in\operatorname{MinCov}(V)}
              \prod_{S\in\mathcal C}w_S,
 \qquad A:=\sum_{\varnothing\ne S\subseteq V}w_S.
\]
Then
\begin{equation}\label{eq:sharp-total-mass-cover-bound}
 Z_V(w)\ge C_k+A-(2^k-1).
\end{equation}
This is best possible for every $A\ge2^k-1$: equality is attained by
\[
 w_V=A-(2^k-2),\qquad w_S=1\quad(S\ne V).
\]
If $k\ge3$, this is the only equality profile.

Consequently, if $n\ge3$ is odd and squarefree and $k=\omega(n)$, then
\begin{equation}\label{eq:squarefree-cumulative-rank-pure-bound}
 \#\{m\in\mathcal M_n:\mu^2(m)=1\}
 \ge C_k+\omega(F_n)-(2^k-1),
\end{equation}
and
\begin{equation}\label{eq:squarefree-cumulative-weighted-excess}
 \mathcal W(n)\ge
 1+\frac{\omega(F_n)-(2^k-1)}{C_k}.
\end{equation}
Among all positive weight profiles with the same total
$\sum_{d\mid n,\ d>1}a(d)=\omega(F_n)$, this conclusion is exact.  The
extremizing profile is an abstract information-theoretic model; it is not
asserted to be realized by Fibonacci exact-rank primes.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Write $w_S=1+x_S$ with $x_S\ge0$.  For every minimal cover $\mathcal C$,
\[
 \prod_{S\in\mathcal C}(1+x_S)
 \ge1+\sum_{S\in\mathcal C}x_S.
\]
Every nonempty $S\subseteq V$ occurs in a minimal cover: use $\{V\}$ when
$S=V$, and otherwise use
$\{S\}\cup\{\{v\}:v\in V\setminus S\}$.  Summing the preceding inequality
over all minimal covers therefore gives
\[
 Z_V(w)\ge C_k+\sum_{\varnothing\ne S\subseteq V}x_S
 =C_k+A-(2^k-1).
\]
The only minimal cover containing $V$ is $\{V\}$.  Hence the displayed
full-support profile has weighted sum
$C_k-1+w_V=C_k+A-(2^k-1)$, proving sharpness.

Suppose $k\ge3$ and $S$ is proper and nonempty. If $|S|=1$, both the
singleton partition of $V$ and $\{S,V\setminus S\}$ are minimal covers
containing $S$. If $|S|\ge2$, choose $s\in S$ and $t\in V\setminus S$; in
addition to the preceding singleton-complement cover, there is the minimal
cover consisting of $S$, $\{s,t\}$, and the singletons
$\{v\}$ for $v\in V\setminus(S\cup\{t\})$. Thus every proper $S$ occurs in
at least two minimal covers. In the summed linear lower bound its coefficient
is therefore at least two, so equality forces $x_S=0$ for every proper $S$.
This proves the equality classification.

For odd squarefree $n$, the nonempty subsets of $\mathcal P(n)$ correspond
to the divisors $d>1$ of $n$.  Exact ranks partition the prime divisors of
$F_n$, and $a(1)=0$, so
\[
 \sum_{\varnothing\ne S\subseteq\mathcal P(n)}a(n_S)
 =\sum_{d\mid n}a(d)=\omega(F_n).
\]
Every weight is positive by exact-rank prime existence.  Apply
\eqref{eq:sharp-total-mass-cover-bound}, then use
Corollary~\ref{cor:canonical-squarefree-slice} and
\eqref{eq:weighted-cover-excess}.
\end{proof}



 succeeded in 224ms:
\section{Birth layers and witness covers}\label{sec:upper-fibers}
%% ====================================================================

The first two lemmas in this section use only rank divisibility and the lcm
identity. They are included to fix the structural framework and are not
presented as new Fibonacci identities. The witness-cover theorem packages
their minimality conditions for the subsequent Fibonacci analysis.
The underlying deletion-one-factor condition is classical: Wagner called
divisor tuples satisfying it minimal multiplicative covers of an integer
\cite{Wagner1978MultiplicativeCovers}, as a multiplicative generalization of
the minimal set covers studied by Hearne--Wagner
\cite{HearneWagner1973MinimalCovers}. Proposition~
\ref{prop:wagner-comparison} states the exact boundary between that skeleton
and the lowered-label structure used here.

\begin{definition}\label{def:birth-layer}
For $n\ge2$ let
\[
D_n:=\operatorname{Div}(F_n),\qquad
B_n:=\{q\in D_n:\ \alpha(q)=n\},\qquad
A(n):=\#B_n.
\]
The set $B_n$ is partially ordered by divisibility. Its set of minimal
elements is
\[
\mathcal M_n:=\{m\in B_n:\ \text{there is no }q\in B_n
\text{ with }q\mid m\text{ and }q<m\},
\]
and
\[
\widthop(B_n):=\max\{\#\mathcal A:\ \mathcal A\subseteq B_n
\text{ is an antichain}\}.
\]
We write $\mathcal P(n)$ for the set of prime divisors of $n$. Since
$F_2=1$, we use the boundary convention
\[
B_2=\mathcal M_2=\varnothing,\qquad A(2)=\widthop(B_2)=0.
\]
\end{definition}

\begin{lemma}[Upper-fiber identity]\label{thm:upper-fiber}
For every $n\ge2$,
\[
B_n=D_n\setminus\bigcup_{p\mid n}D_{n/p}.
\]
Moreover $B_n$ is an upper set in $D_n$: if $q\in B_n$, $r\in D_n$, and
$q\mid r$, then $r\in B_n$. Consequently
\[
B_n=\bigcup_{m\in\mathcal M_n}\{q\in D_n:\ m\mid q\}.
\]
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
By Proposition~\ref{prop:rank-basics}, an integer $q$ lies in $D_m$ if and
only if $\alpha(q)\mid m$. Thus $q\in D_n$ lies outside
$\bigcup_{p\mid n}D_{n/p}$ precisely when $\alpha(q)\mid n$ but
$\alpha(q)\nmid n/p$ for every prime $p\mid n$.

If $\alpha(q)=n$, then clearly $\alpha(q)\nmid n/p$ for every $p\mid n$, so
$q$ is outside the union. Conversely, suppose $\alpha(q)\mid n$ and
$\alpha(q)<n$. Then $n/\alpha(q)>1$; choose a prime $p$ dividing
$n/\alpha(q)$. Since $\alpha(q)\mid n/p$, Proposition~\ref{prop:rank-basics}
gives $q\in D_{n/p}$, contrary to being outside the union. Hence the
complement is exactly the set of $q\in D_n$ with $\alpha(q)=n$, namely
$B_n$. For $n=2$ this also gives
$B_2=D_2\setminus D_1=\varnothing$ under the boundary convention in
Definition~\ref{def:birth-layer}.

Now let $q\in B_n$, $r\in D_n$, and $q\mid r$. Since $r\in D_n$,
$\alpha(r)\mid n$. Since $q\mid r$, every Fibonacci number divisible by $r$
is divisible by $q$; applying the divisibility criterion to
$F_{\alpha(r)}$ gives $\alpha(q)\mid\alpha(r)$. But $\alpha(q)=n$, so
$n\mid\alpha(r)$ and $\alpha(r)\mid n$, whence $\alpha(r)=n$. Therefore
$r\in B_n$.

Finally $D_n$ is finite, so every element $q\in B_n$ has at least one
minimal element $m$ of $B_n$ below it in the divisibility order. The upper-set
property gives the reverse containment: every element of $D_n$ divisible by
some $m\in\mathcal M_n$ lies again in $B_n$. This proves the displayed
decomposition.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Minimal-lcm criterion]\label{thm:minimal-generators}
Let $n\ge2$, and let
$m=\prod_{i=1}^r p_i^{e_i}$ be a divisor of $F_n$, with the $p_i$ distinct
and $e_i\ge1$. Put
\[
\lambda_i:=\alpha(p_i^{e_i}),\qquad
\lambda_i^-:=\alpha(p_i^{e_i-1}),
\]
where $\alpha(1)=1$. Then $m\in\mathcal M_n$ if and only if
\begin{equation}\label{eq:lcm-criterion}
\lcmop(\lambda_1,\ldots,\lambda_r)=n
\quad\text{and}\quad
\lcmop(\lambda_1,\ldots,\lambda_{i-1},\lambda_i^-,
\lambda_{i+1},\ldots,\lambda_r)<n
\quad(1\le i\le r).
\end{equation}
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
Because $m\mid F_n$, Proposition~\ref{prop:rank-basics} gives
$\lambda_i\mid n$ for every $i$. Applying the lcm identity in
Proposition~\ref{prop:rank-basics} repeatedly gives
\[
\alpha(m)=\lcmop(\lambda_1,\ldots,\lambda_r).
\]
Thus the first condition in \eqref{eq:lcm-criterion} is equivalent to
$m\in B_n$.

Assume first that $m\in\mathcal M_n$. Then $m\in B_n$, so the first lcm is
$n$. For a fixed $i$, the integer $m/p_i$ is a proper divisor of $m$ and is
still a divisor of $F_n$. Minimality of $m$ in $B_n$ implies
$m/p_i\notin B_n$. Since $\alpha(m/p_i)\mid n$, this is equivalent to
$\alpha(m/p_i)<n$. A second application of the lcm identity gives
\[
\alpha(m/p_i)=
\lcmop(\lambda_1,\ldots,\lambda_{i-1},\lambda_i^-,
\lambda_{i+1},\ldots,\lambda_r),
\]
which is the lowered prime-power condition in
\eqref{eq:lcm-criterion}.

Conversely, suppose \eqref{eq:lcm-criterion} holds. The first condition gives
$m\in B_n$. Let $q$ be any proper divisor of $m$. Then for at least one
index $i$ the exponent of $p_i$ in $q$ is at most $e_i-1$. Hence
\[
\alpha(q)\mid
\lcmop(\lambda_1,\ldots,\lambda_{i-1},\lambda_i^-,
\lambda_{i+1},\ldots,\lambda_r),
\]
again by the lcm identity and by monotonicity under divisibility of prime
powers. The right-hand side is $<n$ by
\eqref{eq:lcm-criterion}. Since $q\mid m\mid F_n$, we also have
$\alpha(q)\mid n$, and therefore $\alpha(q)<n$. Thus no proper divisor of
$m$ lies in $B_n$, so $m$ is minimal in $B_n$.
\end{proof}

\subsection*{Witness covers}

\begin{definition}\label{def:witness-cover}
An \emph{$n$-witness cover} is a finite set
$\Theta=\{\theta_1,\ldots,\theta_r\}$ of atomic prime powers satisfying:
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*)]
\item $d(\theta)\mid n$ for every $\theta\in\Theta$;
\item the elements of $\Theta$ are pairwise coprime;
\item the full-coordinate supports cover all prime coordinates of $n$,
\[
\bigcup_{\theta\in\Theta}T_n(\theta)=\mathcal P(n);
\]
\item each atom has an essential coordinate not supplied by the other atoms,
\[
E_n(\theta)\nsubseteq
\bigcup_{\eta\in\Theta,\ \eta\ne\theta}T_n(\eta)
\qquad(\theta\in\Theta).
\]
\end{enumerate}
The last condition is the irredundance condition. It is stated with
$E_n$ rather than $T_n$ because overlapping full-coordinate support is
allowed; what is forbidden is that every coordinate lost by lowering
$\theta$ is already supplied at full exponent by the remaining atoms.
\end{definition}

\begin{lemma}[Coordinate test for lowering one atom]
\label{lem:coordinate-lowering-test}
Let $n\ge2$, and let
$\Theta=\{\theta_1,\ldots,\theta_r\}$ be a pairwise coprime set of atomic
prime powers with $d(\theta_i)\mid n$ and
\[
\lcmop(d(\theta_1),\ldots,d(\theta_r))=n.
\]
Fix $\theta_i=p_i^{e_i}$, and put
\[
N_i^-:=\lcmop\bigl(d^-(\theta_i),\ d(\theta_j):j\ne i\bigr).
\]
Then $N_i^-=n$ if and only if
\[
E_n(\theta_i)\subseteq\bigcup_{j\ne i}T_n(\theta_j).
\]
Equivalently, lowering $\theta_i$ to $p_i^{e_i-1}$ keeps the apparition lcm
equal to $n$ exactly when every coordinate lost by the lowering is supplied at
full exponent by some other atom. If
$\ell\in E_n(\theta_i)\setminus\bigcup_{j\ne i}T_n(\theta_j)$, then
$\nu_\ell(N_i^-)<\nu_\ell(n)$, so $N_i^-<n$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
Since $d^-(\theta_i)\mid d(\theta_i)\mid n$ and $d(\theta_j)\mid n$ for
$j\ne i$, the integer $N_i^-$ divides $n$. Hence $N_i^-=n$ is equivalent to
saying that every prime coordinate $\ell\in\mathcal P(n)$ occurs in $N_i^-$
with valuation $\nu_\ell(n)$.

Assume first that
$E_n(\theta_i)\subseteq\bigcup_{j\ne i}T_n(\theta_j)$, and fix
$\ell\in\mathcal P(n)$. Because the original lcm is $n$, at least one of the
numbers $d(\theta_1),\ldots,d(\theta_r)$ supplies the full
$\ell$-adic valuation of $n$. If such a supplier is some $d(\theta_j)$ with
$j\ne i$, then $\ell\in T_n(\theta_j)$ and the same full valuation appears in
$N_i^-$. The only remaining case is that $\theta_i$ itself supplies the full
valuation and no other atom does, so $\ell\in T_n(\theta_i)$ and
$\ell\notin\bigcup_{j\ne i}T_n(\theta_j)$. The assumed containment then gives
$\ell\notin E_n(\theta_i)$. By the definition of $E_n(\theta_i)$,
$\nu_\ell(d^-(\theta_i))=\nu_\ell(n)$, so the lowered factor still supplies the
full $\ell$-coordinate in $N_i^-$. Thus every coordinate of $n$ is still
present at full valuation, and $N_i^-=n$.

Conversely, suppose that some coordinate
$\ell\in E_n(\theta_i)$ is not in $\bigcup_{j\ne i}T_n(\theta_j)$. The
membership $\ell\in E_n(\theta_i)$ says
$\nu_\ell(d^-(\theta_i))<\nu_\ell(n)$. For every $j\ne i$, the exclusion
$\ell\notin T_n(\theta_j)$ says
$\nu_\ell(d(\theta_j))<\nu_\ell(n)$. Therefore the lcm defining $N_i^-$ also
has $\ell$-adic valuation strictly below $\nu_\ell(n)$. Since $N_i^-\mid n$,
this proves $N_i^-<n$.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Wagner comparison and lowered labels]
\label{prop:wagner-comparison}
Let $n\ge2$, and let $\Theta=\{p_1,\ldots,p_r\}$ be a set of distinct prime
atoms with $d_i:=\alpha(p_i)\mid n$. Then $\Theta$ is an $n$-witness cover
if and only if, after forgetting the order of the tuple
$(d_1,\ldots,d_r)$,
\begin{equation}\label{eq:wagner-minimal-multiplicative-cover}
 \lcmop(d_1,\ldots,d_r)=n,
 \qquad
 \lcmop(d_1,\ldots,\widehat{d_i},\ldots,d_r)<n
 \quad(1\le i\le r).
\end{equation}
Thus the prime-atom case is precisely the unordered form of Wagner's minimal
multiplicative cover condition.

For arbitrary pairwise coprime atoms
$\Theta=\{\theta_1,\ldots,\theta_r\}$, the witness condition replaces the
deletion in \eqref{eq:wagner-minimal-multiplicative-cover} by
\begin{equation}\label{eq:lowered-label-multiplicative-cover}
 \lcmop\bigl(d(\theta_1),\ldots,d^-(\theta_i),\ldots,
 d(\theta_r)\bigr)<n
 \quad(1\le i\le r).
\end{equation}
In particular, ladder atoms supply the genuinely additional lowered-label
case: their label is lowered to $d^-(\theta_i)$ rather than deleted.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
For a prime atom $p_i$, one has $d^-(p_i)=\alpha(1)=1$. Hence replacing
$d_i$ by $d^-(p_i)$ in an lcm is identical to omitting $d_i$. Lemma~
\ref{thm:minimal-generators} and Lemma~\ref{lem:coordinate-lowering-test}
then identify the witness-cover conditions with
\eqref{eq:wagner-minimal-multiplicative-cover}. Since the labels form a
minimal cover, no repeated label can occur; forgetting tuple order therefore
produces the corresponding unordered cover without a multiplicity ambiguity.

For arbitrary atoms, the same two lemmas say exactly that the full apparition
lcm is $n$ and that lowering each atom produces
\eqref{eq:lowered-label-multiplicative-cover}. The final assertion is the
distinction between $d^-(p)=1$ for prime atoms and the nontrivial lower rank
of a ladder atom.
\end{proof}

Wagner's condition and its set-theoretic predecessor supply the classical
cover skeleton. The Fibonacci-specific content below is the arithmetic
realization of its labels by exact-rank primes, the lowered-label refinement
forced by prime-power ladders, and the unique correspondence with
$\mathcal M_n$. The canonical squarefree slice and its exact weighted count
in Section~\ref{sec:rank-pure} are further arithmetic consequences of that
realization.

\begin{theorem}\label{thm:witness-classification}
For every $n\ge 2$, multiplication gives a bijection
\[
\Theta\longmapsto \prod_{\theta\in\Theta}\theta
\]
from the set of $n$-witness covers onto $\mathcal M_n$. Its inverse sends
$m\in\mathcal M_n$ to the set
\[
\Theta(m):=\{p^{\nu_p(m)}:p\mid m\}
\]
of prime-power factors occurring in the prime factorization of $m$. In
particular,
\[
\mathcal M_n=\bigl\{\prod_{\theta\in\Theta}\theta:
\Theta\text{ is an $n$-witness cover}\bigr\},
\]
and each element of $\mathcal M_n$ has a unique witness-cover factorization.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Let $m\in\mathcal M_n$, and write its prime factorization as
$m=\prod_{i=1}^r\theta_i$ with $\theta_i=p_i^{e_i}$ and the $p_i$ distinct.
Each $\theta_i$ is atomic: if $\alpha(p_i^{e_i-1})=\alpha(p_i^{e_i})$, then
replacing $\theta_i$ by $p_i^{e_i-1}$ preserves the lcm, contradicting
the minimal-generator criterion in
Lemma~\ref{thm:minimal-generators}. Since
$n=\lcmop(d(\theta_i))$, the sets $T_n(\theta_i)$ cover $\mathcal P(n)$. The
condition $\alpha(m/p_i)<n$ forces
$E_n(\theta_i)\nsubseteq\bigcup_{j\neq i}T_n(\theta_j)$; otherwise the lost
coordinates would be supplied by other factors, keeping the lcm equal to $n$.
Thus $\{\theta_1,\dots,\theta_r\}$ is an $n$-witness cover.

Conversely, let $\Theta$ be an $n$-witness cover and
$m=\prod_{\theta\in\Theta}\theta$. By
the lcm property recorded in Proposition~\ref{prop:rank-basics},
$\alpha(m)=\lcmop(d(\theta):\theta\in\Theta)=n$. For each
$\theta=p^e\in\Theta$, the witness condition
provides $\ell\in E_n(\theta)\setminus\bigcup_{\eta\neq\theta}T_n(\eta)$; at
this coordinate, $\alpha(m/p)<n$.
Lemma~\ref{thm:minimal-generators} then gives $m\in\mathcal M_n$.

It remains to record injectivity and the stated inverse.  If two witness covers
$\Theta$ and $\Theta'$ have the same product, then unique factorization in
$\mathbb Z$ gives the same underlying primes with the same exponents in the two
products.  Since the elements of each witness cover are pairwise coprime prime
powers, those prime-power factors are exactly the elements of the cover.  Hence
$\Theta=\Theta'$.  Therefore multiplication is injective, while the first
paragraph shows that the prime-power factor set $\Theta(m)$ of every
$m\in\mathcal M_n$ is an $n$-witness cover and the second paragraph shows that
every $n$-witness cover maps into $\mathcal M_n$.  Thus multiplication is the
claimed bijection, with inverse $m\mapsto\Theta(m)$.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}\label{cor:support-bound}
Every $m\in\mathcal M_n$ satisfies $\omega(m)\le\omega(n)$.
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
The witness condition provides an injection
$\theta\mapsto\ell(\theta)$ from $\Theta$ into $\mathcal P(n)$: choose
$\ell(\theta)\in E_n(\theta)\setminus\bigcup_{\eta\neq\theta}T_n(\eta)$.
Hence $\omega(m)=\#\Theta\le\omega(n)$.
\end{proof}

\section{Numerical evidence}\label{sec:numerical}
%% ====================================================================

Table~\ref{tab:birth-layer-data-generated} displays the birth layer cardinality $A(n)$
and the number of minimal generators $\#\mathcal M_n$ for $2\le n\le 30$,
computed by exhaustive enumeration. The table is illustrative only: the
unconditional inequalities in Theorem~\ref{article-thm:minimal-generator-growth} do
not rely on this finite sample. Several features visible in the
data agree with the structural results of the preceding sections.
First, $\#\mathcal M_n=1$ whenever $\omega(n)=1$ and
$n\notin\{19,25,27\}$; the exceptions arise from composite Fibonacci numbers
with multiple atomic families at the same prime-power index.
Second, the jump at $n=12$ ($A(12)=10$, $\#\mathcal M_{12}=3$) and at $n=24$
($A(24)=55$, $\#\mathcal M_{24}=4$) reflects the interaction of the
exceptional prime $2$ with the layers having no exact-rank prime at indices
$6$ and $12$.
Third, the first nonempty three-coordinate layer occurs at $n=30$ and has
$\#\mathcal M_{30}=5$; the displayed witness covers realize exactly the
types $\Gamma_1$, $\Gamma_4$, $\Gamma_5$, $\Gamma_7$, and $\Gamma_8$.
The remaining admissible shapes $\Gamma_3$, $\Gamma_6$, and $\Gamma_9$ are
not realized at $n=30$. This exact five-type assertion, including the three
nonrealization proofs, is Proposition~\ref{prop:n30-five-type-classification};
it is not inferred from the table.

The table was produced by the finite divisor/rank computation in the next
proposition, applied exhaustively for $2\le n\le30$.  The manuscript source
includes the short script that performs this enumeration and the generated
table file input below; these files are supplementary to the mathematical
criterion rather than part of the theorem stream. For the classical
factorization literature, see the tables of Brillhart--Montgomery--Silverman
\cite{BrillhartMontgomerySilverman1988Tables}.

\begin{proposition}[Finite divisor/rank verification with supplied factorization]
\label{prop:effective-birth-layer-computation}
Let $n\ge2$ and suppose the integer factorization of $F_n$ is known. Then
$B_n$, $A(n)$, and $\mathcal M_n$ are recovered exactly by the following finite
divisor/rank test.
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\alph*)]
\item Enumerate the divisor set $D_n=\operatorname{Div}(F_n)$ from the
 factorization of $F_n$.
\item Enumerate the divisor set $\operatorname{Div}(n)$. For each
 $q\in D_n$, compute
 \[
 \alpha_n(q):=\min\{d\mid n:q\mid F_d\}.
 \]
\item Set $B_n=\{q\in D_n:q\ge2,\ \alpha_n(q)=n\}$ and
 $A(n)=\#B_n$.
\item Sort $B_n$ by divisibility-compatible order, for instance by size, and
 retain exactly those $m\in B_n$ for which no previously retained element
 divides $m$; the retained set is $\mathcal M_n$.
\end{enumerate}
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
Since every $q\in D_n$ divides $F_n$, Proposition~\ref{article-prop:rank-basics}
shows that $q\mid F_d$ for some $d$ if and only if $\alpha(q)\mid d$. In
particular $q\mid F_n$ implies $\alpha(q)\mid n$, so the least positive index
at which $q$ appears is exactly
\[
\alpha(q)=\min\{d\mid n:q\mid F_d\}=\alpha_n(q).
\]
Hence the test in step (c) gives precisely the definition
$B_n=\{q\in D_n:\alpha(q)=n\}$, with the harmless exclusion of $q=1$ already
consistent with the convention $\alpha(1)=1$ and $n\ge2$. Counting this set
gives $A(n)$.

It remains to justify step (d). The set $B_n$ is finite. If its elements are
processed in increasing size, then every proper divisor of the current element
has already been processed. We claim inductively that every discarded element
has a retained minimal divisor. The claim is vacuous before the first element.
If the current element is discarded, it has a previously retained divisor by
the algorithm, so the claim holds for it; if it is retained, it is its own
retained divisor. Thus every earlier divisor, retained or discarded, has a
retained divisor below it. Consequently the current element is retained if and
only if no smaller element of $B_n$ divides it, which is exactly minimality in
the divisibility poset. The retained set is therefore $\mathcal M_n$.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[Reproducibility scope]
The preceding proposition is used only to document the finite verification
interface for the table, not as a complexity theorem.  After the factorization
of $F_n$ has been supplied, the displayed loops involve only the finite divisor
sets of $F_n$ and $n$, ordinary divisibility tests $q\mid F_d$, and divisor
comparisons inside $B_n$.  The manuscript makes no bit-complexity assertion and
does not include the cost of factoring $F_n$ in the numerical evidence.
\end{remark}

\paragraph{Table generation.}
The reproducibility files
\[
\text{\texttt{artifacts/compute\_birth\_layer\_table.py}},\qquad
\text{\texttt{artifacts/tab\_birth\_layer\_data.tex}}
\]
are included with the manuscript source. Running the command in
\texttt{artifacts/REPRODUCE.md}
\[
\texttt{python artifacts/compute\_birth\_layer\_table.py}
\]
regenerates the table input from the divisor/rank method of
Proposition~\ref{prop:effective-birth-layer-computation}.  The printed table is
therefore an exhaustive enumeration of $A(n)$ and $\#\mathcal M_n$ for
$2\le n\le30$ using only the finite tests proved above; the displayed
factorizations of the small integers $F_n$ are produced by the script's exact
integer trial-division routine. Its complete console output is supplied as
\path{artifacts/birth_layer_table_output.txt}.
\input{generated/tab_birth_layer_data.tex}

\paragraph{Finite verification.}
The supplementary script
\texttt{artifacts/verify\_finite\_claims.py} independently compares the actual
sets obtained by direct divisor/rank enumeration with the upper-fiber and
witness constructions through $n=60$. It
then uses the latter formula through $n=210$: inclusion--exclusion computes
\[
 A(n)=\sum_{S\subseteq\mathcal P(n)}(-1)^{|S|}
 \tau\!\left(F_{n/\prod_{p\in S}p}\right),
\]
and the minimal threshold covers of the conditions
$q\nmid F_{n/p}$ compute $\mathcal M_n$.  The script checks
\eqref{article-eq:finite-minimal-generator-bound}, the simplified bound
$\#\mathcal M_n\le n^{\omega(n)}$, the odd Bell lower bound, and both finite
private-cover bounds in \eqref{article-eq:private-cover-finite-bounds}. It also
computes $R(n)$, checks the pointwise deaggregation bounds
\eqref{article-eq:prime-window-versus-R} and
\eqref{article-eq:pointwise-window-deaggregation}, verifies the exact-rank partition
and \eqref{article-eq:squarefree-blms-pigeonhole} on every squarefree layer, checks
the refined upper bound \eqref{eq:refined-private-coordinate-upper-bound},
checks the fibotomic identities \eqref{article-eq:fibotomic-binet-error} and
\eqref{article-eq:exact-rank-radical-divides-fibotomic}, the entropy inequality
\eqref{article-eq:fibotomic-rank-entropy}, the exact-rank congruence candidate bound,
and the available cases of \eqref{eq:jarden-exact-rank-consequence}, and
records the running ratio against the support-entropy scale
$(\log2)\omega(n)^2/4$. For the rank-pure results, it directly enumerates all
minimal covers for $k\le4$, checks the Hearne--Wagner and connected-component
counts, verifies the M\"obius exact-rank-prime formula on every support, and
tests every canonical rank-pure product against the independently computed
$\mathcal M_n$. This covers all $208$ layers $3\le n\le210$, including exact
realization of every minimal cover on all $104$ odd layers. It reports $59/59$
set equalities for both original comparisons and no finite-bound or rank-pure
failures. On the same $208$ layers it computes the exact total and connected
support spectra from the rank-support hypergraphs and checks both cases of
Theorem~\ref{thm:exact-support-spectra}, as well as the atomic product
formula for the extremal slice. The exact output is
\path{artifacts/finite_verification.txt}. The exact factorizations used for
$2\le n\le210$ are recorded in
\path{artifacts/fibonacci_factorizations_2_210.tsv}; the verifier checks
every row by multiplication. The Python and SymPy versions, commands, file
inventory, and verification scope are in \path{artifacts/REPRODUCE.md}, and
\path{artifacts/SHA256SUMS} authenticates the supplied files. These finite
checks do not provide distributional control of $R(n)$.
In particular, they do not prove the asymptotic hypotheses \textup{(H2)} or
\textup{(BW)}.
