\end{proof}

The rank-pure sector has a canonical description on squarefree indices.  In
particular, it is not then an auxiliary class defined only through the cover
construction.

\begin{corollary}[Canonical squarefree exact-fiber slice]
\label{cor:canonical-squarefree-slice}
Let $n\ge3$ be squarefree.  Then
\begin{equation}\label{eq:canonical-squarefree-slice}
 \mathcal R_n^{\rm rp}
 =\mathcal M_n\cap\{q:\mu^2(q)=1\}
 =\min_{\mid}\{q:\alpha(q)=n,\ \mu^2(q)=1\}.
\end{equation}
Thus the weighted sum in
\eqref{eq:rank-pure-weighted-sector} counts exactly the divisibility-minimal
squarefree elements of the standard exact fiber $\alpha^{-1}(n)$.
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
Proposition~\ref{prop:rank-basics} gives
$B_n=\{q:\alpha(q)=n\}=\alpha^{-1}(n)$.  If a squarefree member of $B_n$
has a proper divisor in $B_n$, that divisor is again squarefree.  Hence
minimality in the squarefree part of the exact fiber is equivalent to
membership in $\mathcal M_n$.

Every element of $\mathcal R_n^{\rm rp}$ is a product of distinct primes and
belongs to $\mathcal M_n$, which proves one inclusion in the first equality.
Conversely, let $m\in\mathcal M_n$ be squarefree.  Its unique atomic
factorization from Theorem~\ref{thm:witness-classification} consists only of
prime atoms $p$.  Put $S=T_n(p)$.  Since $\alpha(p)\mid n$ and $n$ is
squarefree,
\[
 \alpha(p)=\prod_{\ell\in S}\ell=n_S.
\]
Moreover $d^-(p)=1$, so $E_n(p)=T_n(p)=S$.  The witness-cover conditions are
therefore precisely that the distinct supports $S$ form an irredundant cover
of $\mathcal P(n)$.  No such support lies in $\mathcal E_n$, because the
corresponding prime $p$ itself has exact rank $n_S$.  Thus the atomic
factorization of $m$ is one of the uniquely represented products defining
$\mathcal R_n^{\rm rp}$.  This proves the reverse inclusion and the result.
\end{proof}

\subsection*{Worked exact-fiber layers}

\begin{example}[An odd squarefree layer without exceptions]
Let $n=15$ and label its prime coordinates by $V=\{3,5\}$. The relevant
Fibonacci factorizations are
\[
 F_3=2,\qquad F_5=5,\qquad F_{15}=2\cdot5\cdot61,
\]
so
\[
 \Pi_\alpha(3)=\{2\},\qquad
 \Pi_\alpha(5)=\{5\},\qquad
 \Pi_\alpha(15)=\{61\}.
\]
There is no exceptional support. For the prime atoms one has
\[
 T_{15}(2)=E_{15}(2)=\{3\},\quad
 T_{15}(5)=E_{15}(5)=\{5\},\quad
 T_{15}(61)=E_{15}(61)=\{3,5\}.
\]
The two irredundant covers of $V$ are
$\{\{3,5\}\}$ and $\{\{3\},\{5\}\}$. They give the products
\[
 61\qquad\text{and}\qquad2\cdot5=10,
\]
respectively. Thus the canonical squarefree slice is
$\mathcal R_{15}^{\rm rp}=\{10,61\}$, with each atom, support, essential
support, cover, and product visible in the displays.
\end{example}

\begin{example}[An even layer with exceptional supports]
Let $n=6$ and $V=\{2,3\}$. Here the supports $\{2\}$ and $\{2,3\}$ carry
the exceptional ranks $2$ and $6$, respectively. The only nonexceptional
support $\{3\}$ cannot cover $V$, so $\mathcal R_6^{\rm rp}=\varnothing$.
On the other hand, $4$ is a ladder atom with
\[
 d(4)=\alpha(4)=6,\qquad d^-(4)=\alpha(2)=3,
\]
and hence
\[
 T_6(4)=\{2,3\},\qquad E_6(4)=\{2\}.
\]
The singleton $\{4\}$ is a witness cover and its product is $4$. Directly,
$F_6=8$ and the ranks of its divisors $1,2,4,8$ are $1,3,6,6$, so
$\mathcal M_6=\{4\}$. This shows exactly how the exceptional support removes
the prime realization while a lowered-label ladder remains.
\end{example}

\begin{example}[The ladder boundary at $n=91$]
The coordinates are $V=\{7,13\}$. Since $F_7=13$, one has
$\alpha(13)=7$ and $\nu_{13}(F_7)=1$. Proposition~
\ref{prop:prime-power-ladders} therefore gives
\[
 d(13^2)=\alpha(13^2)=13\alpha(13)=91,
 \qquad d^-(13^2)=7.
\]
Consequently

 succeeded in 215ms:
The Fibonacci polynomials were studied by Webb--Parberry
\cite{WebbParberry1969FibonacciPolynomials}; Levy introduced the fibotomic
polynomials and proved their irreducibility over $\mathbb Q$
\cite{Levy2001FibonacciPolynomials}; and Byer--Dvorachek--Eckard--Harrington--
Wise--Wong developed their later systematic theory
\cite{CameronCountsLundMillerPiechnikWong2022Fibotomic}. We use only the
definition and factorization identity recorded in the following lemma.

\begin{lemma}[Fibotomic integer and exact-rank radical]
\label{lem:fibotomic-integer-radical}
Let $d\ge3$, let $\Psi_d(x)$ be the $d$th fibotomic polynomial, and put
\[
 \mathfrak F_d:=\prod_{r\mid d}F_r^{\mu(d/r)}.
\]
Then
\begin{equation}\label{eq:fibotomic-integer-identity}
 \mathfrak F_d=\Psi_d(1)\in\mathbb N,
\end{equation}
and
\begin{equation}\label{eq:exact-rank-radical-divides-fibotomic}
 \prod_{p\in\Pi_\alpha(d)}p\mid\mathfrak F_d.
\end{equation}
For $d=3,5$ the exact-rank radicals are respectively $2$ and $5$; for
$d=6,12$ the exact-rank layer is empty, so the product in the second display
is $1$.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
The polynomial factorization
\[
 F_d(x)=\prod_{r\mid d}\Psi_r(x)
\]
and M\"obius inversion in the multiplicative group of nonzero rational
functions give
\[
 \Psi_d(x)=\prod_{r\mid d}F_r(x)^{\mu(d/r)}.
\]
Evaluation at $x=1$ proves
$\mathfrak F_d=\Psi_d(1)$. The fibotomic polynomial has positive integral
value at $1$, proving \eqref{eq:fibotomic-integer-identity} despite the
negative exponents in the M\"obius expression.

If $p\in\Pi_\alpha(d)$, then $p\mid F_d$ but $p\nmid F_r$ for every proper
divisor $r$ of $d$. In the integral factorization
$F_d=\prod_{r\mid d}\Psi_r(1)$, any divisibility
$p\mid\Psi_r(1)$ with $r<d$ would imply $p\mid F_r$, because
$\Psi_r(1)\mid F_r$. Hence $p\mid\Psi_d(1)=\mathfrak F_d$. Distinct
exact-rank primes are coprime, which proves the radical divisibility without
any appeal to cancellation in the M\"obius quotient. The small-rank claims
follow from $\alpha(2)=3$, $\alpha(5)=5$, and the absence of exact-rank
primes at ranks $6$ and $12$.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Fibotomic rank entropy]
\label{thm:fibotomic-rank-entropy}
Let $\varphi=(1+\sqrt5)/2$, let $\phi_{\rm E}$ denote Euler's totient
function, and, for $d\geq3$, put
\[
 \mathfrak F_d:=\prod_{r\mid d}F_r^{\mu(d/r)}.
\]
For $k\geq0$, set $m=\lfloor k/2\rfloor$ and
\[
 \mathcal H_d(k):=
 k\log\frac{2d}{3}+2\log(m!)+(k-2m)\log(m+1).
\]
There is an absolute constant $C_F$ such that, for every $d\geq3$,
\begin{equation}\label{eq:fibotomic-rank-entropy}
 \mathcal H_d(a(d))\leq \phi_{\rm E}(d)\log\varphi+C_F.
\end{equation}
More generally, every finitely supported family of nonnegative real numbers
$(\lambda_d)_{d\geq3}$ satisfies
\begin{equation}\label{eq:weighted-fibotomic-rank-entropy}
 \sum_{d\geq3}\lambda_d\mathcal H_d(a(d))
 \leq (\log\varphi)\sum_{d\geq3}\lambda_d\phi_{\rm E}(d)
       +C_F\sum_{d\geq3}\lambda_d.
\end{equation}
Uniformly as $d\to\infty$,
\begin{equation}\label{eq:pointwise-exact-rank-bound}
 a(d)\leq
 \left(\frac{\log\varphi}{2}+o(1)\right)
 \frac{\phi_{\rm E}(d)}{\log d}.
\end{equation}
Consequently, uniformly as $n\to\infty$,
\begin{equation}\label{eq:visible-exact-rank-bound}
 A^*(n)\leq
 \left(\frac{\log\varphi}{2}+o(1)\right)
 \frac{\phi_{\rm E}(n)}{\log n}.
\end{equation}
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
Binet's formula, in the form
\[
 F_r=\frac{\varphi^r}{\sqrt5}
 \bigl(1-(-\varphi^{-2})^r\bigr),
\]
and the identities
$\sum_{r\mid d}\mu(d/r)=0$ and
$\sum_{r\mid d}r\mu(d/r)=\phi_{\rm E}(d)$ show that
\begin{equation}\label{eq:fibotomic-binet-error}
 \log\mathfrak F_d=\phi_{\rm E}(d)\log\varphi+E_d,
 \qquad
 |E_d|\leq
 C_F:=\sum_{j\geq1}
 \left|\log\bigl(1-(-\varphi^{-2})^j\bigr)\right|.
\end{equation}
The series defining $C_F$ converges geometrically.

Lemma~\ref{lem:fibotomic-integer-radical} gives the exact-rank radical
divisibility with no cancellation ambiguity.

THE PURPOSE OF THIS QUESTION IS DIFFERENT FROM THE PREVIOUS ROUND, AND YOU MUST BUILD IT
DIFFERENTLY.

The previous round asked him, in effect, "is this good enough for the target journal, and if
not, which journal fits?" That question has a built-in downward bias: it invites him to
locate a lower landing place, and that is what it produced. On the one paper of ours where
the question was instead framed as "this is borderline for the higher tier - what would a
referee at that level object to, and what would secure it?", he produced a specific,
named, reachable threshold, we built exactly that, and he then confirmed the threshold met
and the higher venue defensible.

So this question must be a TIER-RAISING INTERROGATION. Its job is to extract a NAMED,
CONCRETE, REACHABLE mathematical target that would move this paper up a tier - not a venue
recommendation.

The question must contain:

 - the paper's main results stated precisely and completely, with hypotheses, in standard
   language, read from the manuscript;
 - what is imported versus what is ours, honestly separated;
 - the priority position as currently understood, including any prior work he or an earlier
   round identified, stated against our own interest;
 - the machine-verification evidence, and a clear statement that numerical batteries are
   sanity checks and never substitutes for proof;
 - his own previous verdict on this paper, quoted or summarised faithfully, INCLUDING the
   parts unfavourable to us. Read artifacts/oracle_sprint_*_referee.md for it. Do not hide
   the rejection or its grounds.

Then ask him these, in this order:

 1. Name the single most valuable NEW RESULT that would move this paper up a tier. Be
    specific: a theorem statement, not a topic. If several, rank them.
 2. For each, is it REACHABLE FROM THE MACHINERY ALREADY IN THIS PAPER? Say which existing
    lemmas, constructions or estimates would feed it, and what genuinely new ingredient is
    missing. If it is not reachable with what we have, say so plainly and say what would be
    needed instead.
 3. Rank the candidates by success probability times tier impact, and give the probability
    as a number, not a word.
 4. Which of these four standard tier-raising levers applies here, and how:
      (a) settling a named open problem in the literature;
      (b) proving something about the FIELD'S OWN standard objects rather than about
          objects this paper defines;
      (c) removing a hypothesis from an existing theorem, especially one the paper
          currently assumes for convenience;
      (d) a sharpness or matching-bound result showing an existing estimate cannot be
          improved.
 5. What is the strongest objection a referee at the higher tier would raise against the
    paper even after that new result is added?

Constraints you must state to him explicitly:

 - Do NOT answer the tier question by recommending a lower-tier journal. We already know
   where this lands as it stands. We are asking what would move it up.
 - Do NOT propose adding more theorems of the kind already present. An earlier round of
   ours mechanically accumulated results and moved nothing; six of our seven papers were
   then found to have priority omissions, because formally new statements composed from
   standard equivalences carry little independent priority. Say this to him.
 - If the honest answer is that no reachable result exists and this paper cannot rise, say
   that plainly and explain what structural feature caps it. A clear, well-argued ceiling
   is a useful answer and will be acted on. A vague encouragement is not.

Tell him a hard, specific negative is more useful than a soft positive, and that every
objection should come with what would resolve it.

# Submission Metadata

## Journal

- Target journal: The Fibonacci Quarterly
- Submission status: Resubmission following a reject-and-resubmit decision
- Submission components: main article, supplementary material, cover letter,
  and reproducibility materials

## Manuscript

- Title: Minimal preimages of the Fibonacci rank map: squarefree fibers and
  weighted covers
- Main article: `main.pdf` (33 pages)
- Supplement: `supplement.pdf` (36 pages)
- Supplement title: Supplementary material for Weighted witness covers and
  exact-rank multiplicities in Fibonacci apparition fibers
- 2020 Mathematics Subject Classification: 11B39, 11A07, 11A25

## Authors

1. Haobo Ma
   - Affiliation: AELF PTE LTD., #14-02, Marina Bay Financial Centre Tower 1,
     8 Marina Blvd, Singapore 018981, Singapore
   - Email: auric@aelf.io

   - Affiliation: National University of Singapore, Singapore
   - Email: e1327962@u.nus.edu
   - Corresponding author: yes

## Referee-Facing Submitted Files

1. `main.pdf`: 33-page primary manuscript.
2. `supplement.pdf`: 36-page supplementary material containing connected
   factorizations, support classifications, secondary consequences, finite
   checks, and reproducibility commentary.
3. `cover_letter.txt`: resubmission cover letter.
4. `submission_metadata.md`: this submission manifest.
5. Verification scripts:
   `artifacts/compute_birth_layer_table.py`,
   `artifacts/verify_finite_claims.py`,
   `scripts/verify_deepening_delta.py`, and
   `scripts/verify_squarefree_slice.py`.
6. Unit tests:
   `artifacts/test_verify_finite_claims.py`,
   `scripts/test_verify_deepening_delta.py`, and
   `scripts/test_verify_squarefree_slice.py`.
7. Archived verification outputs and data:
   `artifacts/birth_layer_table_output.txt`,
   `artifacts/finite_verification.txt`,
   `artifacts/deepening_delta_verification.txt`,
   `artifacts/squarefree_slice_verification.txt`,
   `artifacts/fibonacci_factorizations_2_210.tsv`, and
   `artifacts/tab_birth_layer_data.tex`.
8. Literature and named-problem audits:
   `artifacts/literature_check.md` and
   `artifacts/named_problem_audit.md`.
9. Reproduction and integrity files:
   `artifacts/REPRODUCE.md`, `artifacts/SHA256SUMS`, and `.gitattributes`.

## Claims And Priority Boundary

The claimed new contribution is the lowered-label Fibonacci realization of
the witness-cover structure, its unique correspondence with the
divisibility-minimal exact fiber, the canonical squarefree slice, and its
exact arithmetic weights. The article also gives prime inverse rays and uses
them to answer the two FitzGibbons-Javaheri-Miller-Verga problems in the
stronger prime form.

The classical minimal-cover skeleton is imported: Wagner's minimal
multiplicative covers give the prime-atom case, and Hearne-Wagner minimal set
covers give its set-theoretic predecessor. Classical exact-rank prime
existence supplies every prime used in the inverse-ray application, so no new
prime-existence theorem is claimed. The fixed-point classification and orbit
termination used there are also imported, from Marques and Luca-Tron,
respectively. The article records other classical rank, lifting, primitive
divisor, and fibotomic inputs at their points of use.

## Resubmission Record

The article now contains numbered statements for classical prime-power rank
lifting, the prime-ladder alternative, and the ladder-slot formulas used by
the upper bounds. It also contains a numbered proposition delimiting Wagner's
classical condition from the lowered-label generalization. H1 and H2 are
identified only as sufficient maximum-window hypotheses; Corollary 6.9 is
purely conditional, no positive arithmetic evidence for either hypothesis is
claimed, and the Bugeaud-Luca-Mignotte-Siksek conjecture is recorded as
predicting that both fail.


 succeeded in 161ms:
Editor
The Fibonacci Quarterly

Dear Editor,

We resubmit "Minimal preimages of the Fibonacci rank map: squarefree fibers
and weighted covers" to The Fibonacci Quarterly following a reject-and-resubmit
decision.

For each positive integer n, the article studies the exact fiber of the
Fibonacci rank-of-apparition map and its divisibility-minimal elements. Its
main contribution is an exact classification of those minimal elements by
irredundant witness covers, together with the canonical squarefree slice and
its exact weighted cover count. The article also settles two problems posed by
FitzGibbons, Javaheri, Miller, and Verga: whether every fixed-point-order class
of positive order contains infinitely many relatively prime integers, and
whether the basin of every fixed point x > 5 contains infinitely many
relatively prime integers. Both are answered in the stronger prime form as a
direct corollary of classical exact-rank prime existence; no new
prime-existence input is claimed.

The resubmission addresses the two structural grounds for the decision.
First, the affected upper-bound arguments are now independently checkable
within the article. It contains the numbered proposition "Classical
prime-power rank lifting," a numbered lemma establishing the prime-ladder
alternative, and the unique ladder-slot formulas needed by those arguments.
The relevant dependencies now point to numbered statements in the article. To
accommodate these proofs, the article has increased from 27 to 33 pages and
from 24 to 31 theorem, lemma, proposition, and corollary environments.

Second, the priority boundary has been corrected. The article now cites Carl
G. Wagner, "Minimal multiplicative covers of an integer," Discrete Mathematics
24 (1978), 87-94, and T. Hearne and C. Wagner, "Minimal covers of finite
sets," Discrete Mathematics 5 (1973), 247-251. A numbered comparison
proposition states that the prime-atom case is the unordered form of Wagner's
condition and identifies the additional structure as the lowering of a
prime-power rank label rather than deletion of that label. The classical
minimal-cover skeleton is not claimed as new.

The former H1 and H2 conjectural language has also been corrected. H1 and H2
are now called sufficient maximum-window hypotheses. Corollary 6.9 is stated
purely conditionally, records that no positive arithmetic evidence for either
hypothesis is presently known, and states that the published
Bugeaud-Luca-Mignotte-Siksek conjecture predicts that both hypotheses fail.

The submission package includes the 33-page article, the 36-page supplement,
and the verification scripts, tests, archived outputs, literature audit,
checksums, and reproduction instructions listed in the accompanying metadata.
We ask that the supplement and computational materials be made available to
the referee.

Sincerely,

Haobo Ma
AELF PTE LTD.

Singapore 018981, Singapore
auric@aelf.io

Wenlin Zhang
National University of Singapore

 succeeded in 153ms:
总体判断
确有一个截至本次公开检索仍未见解决、并可由现成输入直接闭合的外部问题簇。 最强表述不是原问题所要求的“无穷多个互素整数”，而是：

对每个正固定点阶 k≥1，存在无穷多个素数具有固定点阶 k；对秩映射的每个固定点 x>5，其吸引域包含无穷多个素数。

这同时严格加强并解决 FitzGibbons–Javaheri–Miller–Verga 的两个未编号问题。不过，必须准确评价其方法论含义：该结论只消耗稿件机器的最薄一层——精确秩素数存在性及单边全支撑扇区——而不需要加权覆盖渐近、连通分块或 fibotomic 熵。它是一个真实的具名问题解答，但单独成文的期刊上限仍是专业整数序列／初等数论期刊，不会仅凭此把论文提升至高层级解析数论期刊。
按“成功概率 × 可发表层级影响”排序，我只建议以下三项；第四、第五项不应牵强补足。
排名文献问题当前判断成功概率合理期刊层级1固定点阶层与吸引域中的无穷互素族可直接加强为无穷素数并解决数学闭合约 99%；综合新颖性约 85%−90%Fibonacci Quarterly, Integers, Journal of Integer Sequences2最小固定点阶元素及最终前驱猜想可作精确最优化归约；尚缺值域算术非平凡推进 20%−30%；完整解决 5%−10%推进：专业期刊；完整解决可至 Research in Number Theory／JNT3Luca–Tron 自 Fibonacci 除数计数问题有新的结构桥，但缺“小精确秩素数”输入改善既有下界 5%−10%；完整预测 <2%强部分结果可至 JNT／Acta Arithmetica

1. 首选：素数反向射线定理，同时解决两个已明确提出的问题
1.1 文献定位
FitzGibbons、Javaheri、Miller、Verga 的论文 Dynamics of the Fibonacci Order of Appearance Map 最终期刊版本于 2025 年 12 月 31 日在线发表。作者在第 1 节、文内第 3 页询问，对每个 k≥1，是否存在 “infinitely many relatively prime integers whose fixed point order is k”；又在第 3 节、文内第 7 页猜想，对每个固定点 x>5，有 “Ωx​ contains infinitely many relatively prime elements”。两处均为未编号问题／猜想。Taylor & Francis Online+2威廉姆斯学院+2
为避免与稿件的秩函数 α 混淆，下文记
h(m):=min{j≥0:α∘j(m) 是 α 的固定点},
Λk​:={m:h(m)=k},Ωx​:={m:α∘j(m)=x 对某个 j≥0}.
该动力学论文所引用并重新证明的背景是：固定点恰为 5a 与 12⋅5a，所有轨道最终到达固定点，因而不存在长度大于一的周期轨道。威廉姆斯学院
1.2 可直接写入论文的定理
定理 A：秩映射的素数反向射线


对每个 y≥7、y=12，存在两两不同的素数序列
p1​,p2​,…
使得
α(p1​)=y,α(pj+1​)=pj​(j≥1).


对每个固定点 x>5，集合
Ωx​∩P
是无穷集。因此 Ωx​ 包含无穷多个两两互素整数。


对每个 k≥1，集合
Λk​∩P
是无穷集。因此 Λk​ 包含无穷多个两两互素整数。


还可附加定量形式。令
E0​(t)=t,Er+1​(t)=φEr​(t).
上述素数可选得
pr​<Er​(y).
特别地，每个 Ωx​ 中不超过 X 的素数个数至少具有
log∗X−Ox​(1)
的下界；而对固定 k，
#{p≤X:p∈Λk​}≥#{b≥1:Ek​(12⋅5b)≤X},
从而至少具有一个 k+1 重迭代对数级下界。


证明证书
对每个 d≥3、d∈/{6,12}，Carmichael–Bilu–Hanrot–Voutier 原始因子定理给出素数 p 满足 α(p)=d；这一输入已经被稿件明确记录。
从 y≥7, y=12 开始，第一次所取的精确秩素数不可能是 2,3,5，故仍为至少 7 的素数，也不可能等于异常合数 12。因此可无限递归。若所得素数发生重复，则出现一个长度大于一的周期轨道，或出现素数固定点；前者与无非平凡周期矛盾，后者只能是 5，而构造从不进入 5。这证明第一项。
对于固定点 x=12, x>5，直接令 y=x。对于 x=12，利用
7⟼8⟼6⟼12
并在 7 上方建立素数反向射线。于是每个 Ωx​ 都含无穷素数。
最后，对每个 b≥1 取不同固定点
xb​=12⋅5b,
并在 xb​ 上方建立长度 k 的素数链
pk,b​⟼pk−1,b​⟼⋯⟼p1,b​⟼xb​.
所有中间点都是非固定素数，故 h(pk,b​)=k。不同 b 所得顶端素数不同，因为施行 α∘k 后分别得到不同的 xb​。
定量上，所取素数整除 Fd​，所以
p≤Fd​<φd,
递归即得 pr​<Er​(y)。
1.3 现稿机制的具体分工


机制 1：保证精确秩等式而非仅秩整除；每一步确实落在单一精确纤维。


机制 3：以 a(d)>0 的形式提供非异常秩的素数选择。


机制 4：每一步正是全支撑单边覆盖 {P(d)} 所对应的一原子 rank-pure 极小元。稿件已经证明所选素数的支持超图得到逐点保留。


机制 10：可对有限初始段给出可复核秩证书，但不参与无限性证明。


机制 2 与 5–9 均不需要。这一点并非缺陷，而是重要的能力诊断：该具名问题的难度来自原作者没有沿精确秩素数向后迭代，而非来自精确秩重数分布。
1.4 仍缺的最小新输入
数学上不缺新输入。只缺两项出版前工作：


完整的新颖性审计；


将“存在”升级为上述定量计数，并整理异常结点 6,12 的统一表述。


本次已检索最终论文、arXiv、精确短语及公开可见引用页面，未发现后续解答；但这不等同于完成 MathSciNet、zbMATH、Google Scholar 全引用图谱与作者通信。因此：

[文献状态未完全核验] 截至 2026 年 8 月 15 日的公开检索未发现解答，但尚未向四位作者确认，也未完成所有订阅数据库的人工引用审计。

1.5 具体证伪检查
此建议可由以下任一项否定：


找到已发表文献明确证明某个 Λk​ 或每个 Ωx​ 含无穷素数；


找到递归过程中可能进入 6 或 12 的素数目标——这是不可能的，因为后续目标本身为素数；


找到两个构造素数重复但不产生周期轨道；


发现动力学中存在长度大于一的周期轨道；


发现某个 d∈/{1,2,6,12} 无精确秩素数。


第 2–5 项分别与素合数性、函数迭代、无非平凡周期定理及原始因子定理直接冲突。故主要风险在文献新颖性，而非数学逻辑。
1.6 期刊层级
单独作为短文，合理目标是 Fibonacci Quarterly、Integers 或 Journal of Integer Sequences。加入当前稿件，则适合作为一个“inverse dynamics application”节，显著改善外部问题关联，但不足以单独把论文提升到 Journal of Number Theory 或 Acta Arithmetica 层级。综合成功概率估计为 85%−90%。

2. 次选：最小固定点阶元素与最终前驱猜想
2.1 文献定位
同一论文第 1 节、文内第 3 页询问如何改进
λk​:=minΛk​
的上下界；第 2 节、文内第 5 页提出未编号猜测：“it is probably true that z(λk+1​)=λk​” 对充分大的 k 成立。论文仅列出
1,6,4,3,2,11,89,1069,2137,4273,59833,…
这一初始段。威廉姆斯学院+1
本次公开检索未发现后续证明；同样未完成作者通信与订阅数据库全量核查。
2.2 当前机器能够给出的精确归约
定义最小精确前像
β(n):=min{q:α(q)=n}.
由于每个精确纤维元素都在整除序下位于某个极小元之上，
β(n)=minMn​.
于是对 k≥1 有严格恒等式
λk+1​=n∈Λk​min​β(n).
因此最终前驱猜想等价于
β(λk​)=n∈Λk​min​β(n)(k≫1).​
这不是形式性重述：稿件的唯一原子分解把 β(n) 变为一个带实际整数成本的最小不可约覆盖问题。每个候选原子 θ 赋成本 logθ，则
logβ(n)=Θ 为 n-witness covermin​θ∈Θ∑​logθ.
现稿已经证明 witness cover 与 Mn​ 的双射及分解唯一性。
2.3 机制分工


机制 1：把最小前像转化为原子乘积的有限最优化。


机制 2：把每个槽分成精确秩素数槽与单坐标 ladder 槽；Hall 条件处理互素选择。


机制 3：给出各素数槽的候选集合及精确基数。


机制 4：把 rank-pure 部分变成最小权不可约覆盖／最小横截集问题。


机制 5：连通分解可用于分支定界；k≤4 分类给出小支撑终止规则。


机制 10：可以产出 β(n) 与 λk​ 的有限可核验证书。


2.4 最小的新算术输入
现稿控制的是
a(d)=#{p:α(p)=d},
而最小值问题需要控制
Pα​(d):=min{p:α(p)=d}
以及各 ladder 原子的实际大小。重数与最小元素是正交信息：即使 a(d) 很大，也可能所有精确秩素数都很大；即使 a(d)=1，唯一素数也可能异常小。
因此最小且不可替代的新输入是一个移动秩的最小原子成本定理。可执行的中间靶标是定义
cn​(I,J):=θ∈AI,J​(n)min​logθ,
并证明一个足以推出
MinCost(λk​)<MinCost(n)(n∈Λk​, n=λk​)
的统一成本分离不等式。现有 fibotomic 乘积上界只控制所有精确秩素数的总乘积，不能提供这种最小值分离。
2.5 证伪检查


有限反例检索：精确计算下一批 λk​ 及 α(λk+1​)。任一失败即可否定“对所有 k”版本，但不能否定“充分大 k”版本。


参数化反例：构造无穷多个
nk​∈Λk​,nk​>λk​,β(nk​)<β(λk​).
这会彻底否定最终前驱猜想。


信息充分性检查：寻找两个目标秩，它们具有相同的支持谱与原子槽基数，却具有反向的最小原子成本排序。若存在，则证明现有“计数机器”在不加入数值成本输入时不可能决定 λk​。


计算项目：对 λ11​,…,λ15​ 建立完整候选排除证书，而不只计算一个较小候选。
[数值未核验] 本次没有执行这一分解与证书计算。


2.6 成功概率与层级
仅靠当前机器证明最终猜想的概率约 5%−10%。得到新的精确递归、显著扩展经证明的 λk​ 表、或者建立非平凡上下界的概率约 20%−30%。
纯有限表适合 Fibonacci Quarterly 或实验数学方向；若获得真正的统一最小原子成本定理并证明最终前驱关系，则可达到 Research in Number Theory，甚至 Journal of Number Theory。目前尚无理由预期这一成本定理可由现有 fibotomic 信息推出。

3. 第三候选：Luca–Tron 自 Fibonacci 除数计数问题
3.1 文献定位
Luca 与 Tron 在 The Distribution of Self-Fibonacci Divisors 中研究
A:={N:N∣FN​},A(X):=#(A∩[1,X]).
论文引言、文内第 2 页提出 Pomerance 型预测
logA(X)=logX−(1+o(1))loglogXlogXlogloglogX​.
第 4 节、文内第 7–8 页明确指出：“the problem of finding lower bounds for A(x) requires completely different ideas”，并把困难归结为构造许多具有较小 c(n) 的平方自由整数及共享 Pratt–Fibonacci 树。论文最终发表于 Fields Institute Communications 77（2015），第 149–158 页。施普林格自然+3arXiv+3arXiv+3
[文献状态未完全核验] 本次检索未发现该 Pomerance 型预测的证明，但该问题年代较久，未完成 MathSciNet／zbMATH 的逐篇引用审查，因此其“仍完全开放”的置信度低于前两项。
3.2 当前机器给出的新结构桥
若 d∈A，则
α(d)∣d.
若又取 m∈Md​，则 α(m)=d。令
N=lcm(d,m).
由秩的最小公倍数恒等式，
α(N)=lcm(α(d),α(m))=lcm(α(d),d)=d.
因为 d∣N，故 α(N)∣N，于是
N∣FN​.
因此得到无条件桥接命题：
d∣Fd​, m∈Md​⟹lcm(d,m)∣Flcm(d,m)​.​
秩的最小公倍数恒等式是稿件结构的基础。
当 (d,m)=1 时，输出为 dm，且 m↦dm 单射。因此，一个自 Fibonacci 指标 d 上的大量、小尺寸、与 d 互素的极小精确秩核心，可以直接膨胀为大量新的自 Fibonacci 除数。
3.3 机制分工


机制 1：给出上述 lcm 桥及极小精确秩核心。


机制 2：用 Hall 条件选择与 d 互素的原子，避免 lcm 碰撞。


机制 3：精确计算 rank-pure 核心的加权数目。


机制 4：提供
exp(4log2​ω(d)2+O(ω(d)))
级的潜在核心数。


机制 5：连通分解可分析不同核心产生同一 lcm 的碰撞。


机制 6：给出所有候选精确秩素数的总乘积容量限制。


机制 8：同时警告，ω(Fd​) 的总质量不能保证这些核心分散在许多小秩槽中。


机制 9：若能验证相应分块窗口条件，可控制连通核心的主导性。


稿件的加权 rank-pure 精确公式及支持保真性正是这里所需的组合部分。
3.4 最小的新输入
所需输入不是更多的核心个数，而是大量核心的尺寸截断。一个足够明确的中间靶标是：

存在常数 c,C>0 及足够丰富的平方自由 d∈A，使得
#{m∈Rdrp​:(m,d)=1, m≤dC}≥exp(cω(d)2).(SF)

一旦有 (SF)，上述 lcm 桥会把覆盖熵转化为 A(X) 的实际下界。
(SF) 所需的最小新算术内容是：
在许多同时出现的秩 dS​ 上选择满足 pS​≤dSO(1)​ 的精确秩素数。
现有结论至多给出 pS​≤FdS​​，即
logpS​=O(dS​),
其尺度远大于将覆盖计数转化为 A(X) 所需的多项式或准多项式尺寸。故这一候选虽有真正的新桥接结构，却仍受一个与现稿重数问题不同的“小精确秩素数”障碍阻断。
3.5 证伪检查


对已知平方自由自 Fibonacci 指标 d，计算
Qd​(T)=#{m∈Rdrp​:(m,d)=1,logm≤T},
并检验 T=Clogd 时是否保留 ecω(d)2 级核心。


计算最小成本
Cmin​S∈C∑​logPα​(dS​).
若它在所有可构造 d 上均远大于任意 O(logd)，则 (SF) 路线失效。


若可证明对几乎所有相关 d，每个 rank-pure 核心均满足
logm≫dδ
对某个 δ>0，则该方法不可能改善 Luca–Tron 所需尺度。


检查 lcm 碰撞：若绝大多数核心都因与 d 共享素因子而压缩到少量相同输出，Hall 型互素填充不足，桥接计数即失效。
[数值未核验] 本次未进行这些大整数分解与截断统计。


3.6 成功概率与层级
lcm 桥本身几乎确定成立，但它单独不是高层级结果。借此改善已知 A(X) 下界的概率约 5%−10%；达到 Pomerance 型预测的概率低于 2%。
若能证明 (SF) 或得到明显改善现有指数的下界，可达到 Journal of Number Theory 或 Acta Arithmetica。现稿机器本身不提供完成 (SF) 的尺寸信息。

能力上限
不存在值得列为第四、第五项的同等候选。结构性原因可以精确表述如下。
1. 机器是“固定目标秩内部”的，而主要高层级问题是“移动秩之间”的
现稿能够在给定 n 后：
分类 α−1(n) 的极小元,枚举支持模式,计算各模式的重数.
它不能控制随 n 变化的
a(d),d∣n,
在同一个典型除数格上的联合分布。稿件自身已经把正常阶问题精确隔离为加权覆盖超额与非 rank-pure 补扇区的共同上界，并指出现有秩整除、固定出现指数及原始因子结果均不能提供该移动精确纤维分布。
2. 机器控制“有多少选择”，不控制“选择有多小”
a(d)
与加权覆盖和是基数信息；最小固定点阶元素和自 Fibonacci 除数计数需要
Pα​(d)=min{p:α(p)=d}
及其多秩同步尺寸。fibotomic 约束给出总乘积容量，却不提供最小素数或低分位数。稿件关于固定总质量时全支撑集中极小化覆盖和的定理进一步证明：总原始因子质量不能替代分散信息。
3. 支持超图不携带三类外部算术
现有支撑机器没有编码：
移动 Chebotarev／筛条件,p-进提升异常,α(N+i) 的加性同步.
因此，它可以解决“是否存在无限反向轨道”这类乘法存在性问题，却不能自然推进素数同余极纤维、Wall–Sun–Sun 型条件、奇赋值问题或局部排序猜想。
4. 当前最强的内部未解目标不是外部具名问题
稿件真正暴露出的高价值算术接口仍是：
logW(n)=o((loglogn)2)
的正常阶及非 rank-pure 扇区的匹配上界。现稿明确说明，这两部分共同构成目标渐近的必要缺口，而且 ω(Fn​) 的累积信息不足以关闭它。
它是一个自然且重要的新问题，但目前属于本文所提出的接口，不应包装成既有文献中的命名开放问题。

最终裁决


应立即写入的最强外部定理：素数反向射线定理。它同时解决 2025 年文献中的固定点阶互素问题与吸引域互素猜想，并严格加强为无穷素数。


最值得继续投资的内部研究方向：不是继续扩展有限支撑分类，而是研究精确秩原子的两个外部算术剖面：
联合重数分散与最小原子尺寸.


能力上限：除上述动力学存在性问题外，当前机器尚不能触及同等或更高层级的命名解析数论问题。其障碍不是覆盖组合学不足，而是缺少移动精确秩素数的联合分布、尺寸统计与 p-进／筛论输入。


 succeeded in 157ms:
本轮提出的素数逆向射线定理及其两个动力系统推论已经过独立核验并纳入正文：对每个正固定点阶 (k\geq1)，相应阶层含有无穷多个素数；对每个固定点 (x>5)，其吸引域含有无穷多个素数。该结论确实严格加强并解决 FitzGibbons--Javaheri--Miller--Verga 的两个未编号问题，因为无穷多个互异素数自动构成两两互素族。后续不得重复该构造、其迭代指数定量界或 (12) 的例外路径。

关于最小前驱函数的全局定义，原陈述不成立。由于 (F_2=1)，故 (alpha^{-1}(2)=\varnothing)，从而 (eta(2)=\min\{q:\alpha(q)=2\}) 无定义；同时 (2\in\Lambda_4)，所以对所有 (n\in\Lambda_k) 直接取 (eta(n)) 的递推公式在 (k=4) 已经失去意义。若重新提出该方向，必须首先在 (operatorname{im}\alpha) 上严格限定定义域，或在空纤维处取 (+\infty)，并证明修正后的命题；然而非空精确纤维上的 (eta(n)=\min\mathcal M_n) 以及相应的最小见证覆盖代价公式，已由本文的上纤维定理与见证覆盖分类直接蕴含，不得作为新结果再次提出。

关于自 Fibonacci 除数的最小公倍数桥，该推论不得作为本文的新定理。Luca--Tron《The Distribution of Self-Fibonacci Divisors》Lemma 1.3 已明确给出 (z(\operatorname{lcm}(a,b))=\operatorname{lcm}(z(a),z(b)))，结合 (n\mid F_n\iff z(n)\mid n)，立即推出所述桥式结论。可以引用该结论，但不得重复其公开推理过程或改名重述。

下一轮仅研究如下一个核心开放问题：无条件证明
\[
 \log\#\mathcal M_n\sim\frac{\log2}{4}(\log\log n)^2
\]
对几乎所有正整数 (n) 成立。所提交结果必须同时闭合当前证明中的两个必要接口：其一，对几乎所有奇数 (n) 建立足以推出
\[
 \log\mathcal W(n)=o((\log\log n)^2)
\]
的加权精确秩素数离散估计；其二，将非秩纯见证覆盖的总贡献无条件控制在
\(exp(o((\log\log n)^2))\) 的相对尺度内。不得以单个固定秩、累计质量 (omega(F_n))、点态上界 (a(d))、有限计算或条件假设替代上述几乎处处的同步控制。要求给出完整、可审查、达到发表标准的定理与证明；若该目标确实不能由现有公开输入闭合，则必须精确识别一个在逻辑上充分且目前文献中不存在的算术命题，并证明其与所缺 normal-order 上界之间的严格蕴含关系，但不得提交增量式重述或中间过程结论。

不要重复之前内容.不要重复其他人已经发表公开的推理过程(定理、推论、猜想、命题及证明), 可以使用其他人的结论并引用.不要中间过程结论.请使用学术化语言表达, 禁止口语.

diff --git a/tools/chatgpt-oracle/sprint/ask/A7_tier.txt b/tools/chatgpt-oracle/sprint/ask/A7_tier.txt

index 0000000000000000000000000000000000000000..a69bc17ebb9f45641422737e3ea47e967d2f26d3
--- /dev/null
+++ b/tools/chatgpt-oracle/sprint/ask/A7_tier.txt
@@ -0,0 +1,173 @@
+We need a tier-raising mathematical assessment of the attached manuscript, not another journal-fit assessment. The paper is currently a reconstructed resubmission to The Fibonacci Quarterly after a reject-and-resubmit decision. We already know the plausible landing level of the paper as it stands. The question is: what single, named, concrete, realistically reachable new theorem would raise its mathematical level by one tier?
+
+Please use the following account as the claim and priority boundary, while checking every statement against main.pdf.
+
+Definitions and main results proved in the manuscript
+
+Let F_0=0, F_1=1, and let alpha(q) be the least r>=1 for which q divides F_r, with alpha(1)=1. For n>=2 let B_n=alpha^{-1}(n) and let M_n be the divisibility-minimal elements of B_n; B_2=M_2 is empty. Write P(n) for the prime divisors of n, omega(n)=#P(n), Pi_alpha(d)={p prime: alpha(p)=d}, and a(d)=#Pi_alpha(d).
+
+1. Exact classification of the minimal exact fiber. For a prime power theta=p^e set d(theta)=alpha(p^e) and d^-(theta)=alpha(p^{e-1}); call theta atomic when d^-(theta)<d(theta). If d(theta) divides n, define its full support
+
+   T_n(theta)={ell in P(n): nu_ell(d(theta))=nu_ell(n)}
+
+and its essential support
+
+   E_n(theta)={ell in T_n(theta): nu_ell(d^-(theta))<nu_ell(n)}.
+
+An n-witness cover is a finite pairwise-coprime set Theta of atomic prime powers such that every d(theta) divides n, the sets T_n(theta) cover P(n), and every theta has an essential coordinate absent from all other full supports:
+
+   E_n(theta) is not contained in the union of T_n(eta) over eta!=theta.
+
+For every n>=2, multiplication Theta -> product(theta in Theta) is a bijection from n-witness covers to M_n, with inverse given by the prime-power factors of m. Hence every m in M_n has a unique atomic factorization and omega(m)<=omega(n). Equivalently, if m=product p_i^{e_i} divides F_n, then m lies in M_n exactly when lcm_i alpha(p_i^{e_i})=n and replacing any alpha(p_i^{e_i}) by alpha(p_i^{e_i-1}) makes the lcm strictly smaller than n.
+
+2. Complete Fibonacci prime-power realization used in that classification. If z_p=alpha(p) and, for p not in {2,5}, h_p=nu_p(F_{z_p}), then
+
+   alpha(2)=3, alpha(4)=6, alpha(2^e)=3*2^{e-2} for e>=3;
+   alpha(5^e)=5^e for e>=1;
+   alpha(p^e)=p^{max(e-h_p,0)}z_p for p not in {2,5}.
+
+Thus the atoms are 2, 4, 2^e for e>=4 (8 is not atomic), every 5^e, every prime p not in {2,5}, and p^e for p not in {2,5} when e>h_p. A prime atom has E_n=T_n; a nonprime ladder atom has E_n either empty or the singleton containing its underlying prime. For each nonempty support pair I subseteq J subseteq P(n), the atomic family A_{I,J}(n) is either the prime window consisting of the disjoint union of exact-rank prime classes with full support J (only when I=J), or a ladder family with I={p}; each labelled ladder slot has at most one explicit candidate. These statements make every atomic family finite and make the upper bounds independently checkable.
+
+3. Canonical rank-pure and squarefree sectors. For n>=3, V=P(n), nonempty S subseteq V, put
+
+   n_S=product(ell in S) ell^{nu_ell(n)},  a_n(S)=a(n_S),
+
+and exclude the at most two supports for which n_S is 2, 6, or 12. For every irredundant cover C of V by distinct nonempty, nonexceptional subsets, choose p_S in Pi_alpha(n_S). The products product_{S in C}p_S form a sector R_n^rp contained in M_n, uniquely represented, with exact count
+
+   #R_n^rp = sum_C product_{S in C} a(n_S),
+
+where
+
+   a(n_S)=sum_{d|n_S} mu(n_S/d) omega(F_d).
+
+This sector is canonically in bijection with the inclusion-minimal transversals of the coordinate hypergraph whose vertices are pairs (S,p), p in Pi_alpha(n_S), and whose coordinate edge for ell consists of the pairs with ell in S. If n>=3 is squarefree, then
+
+   R_n^rp = {m in M_n: m is squarefree}
+          = the divisibility-minimal squarefree elements of alpha^{-1}(n).
+
+For odd n no support is exceptional, so every irredundant cover is realized. If C_k is the number of irredundant covers of a labelled k-set, then #M_n>=C_k and
+
+   C_k ~ vartheta_{k mod 2} binom(k,floor(k/2)) 2^{k^2/4},
+   log C_k=(log 2)k^2/4+O(k).
+
+With
+
+   W(n)=C_k^{-1} sum_C product_{S in C}a(n_S),  k=omega(n),
+
+one has, on odd n,
+
+   log #R_n^rp=(log 2)k^2/4+log W(n)+O(k),  W(n)>=1.
+
+Consequently the desired almost-all formula
+
+   log #M_n ~ (log 2)/4 (log log n)^2
+
+would necessarily require log W(n)=o((log log n)^2) outside o(x) odd integers n<=x. This condition is not known to be sufficient, because ladder and other non-rank-pure covers must also be bounded; already 169 lies in M_91 but outside R_91^rp.
+
+4. Unconditional counting bounds, with the unresolved arithmetic term displayed rather than hidden. Let R(n) be the maximum of 1 and the cardinalities of the atomic families A_{I,J}(n). For n>=3, M_n is nonempty. If k=omega(n)>=3 and r=floor(k/2), then
+
+   (2^r-1)^{k-r} <= #M_n
+   <= sum_{s=1}^k binom(k,s)(2R(n))^s 2^{s(k-s)}.
+
+Therefore, uniformly when omega(n)->infinity,
+
+   (log 2)omega(n)^2/4-O(1)
+   <= log #M_n
+   <= (log 2)omega(n)^2/4+omega(n)log R(n)+O(omega(n)).
+
+The paper does not claim an unconditional asymptotic or an exact quadratic coefficient for the full M_n. It also proves the coarser bounds #M_n<=n^{omega(n)}, limsup log(#M_n)log log n/(log n)^2<=1, and
+
+   ((log 2)/4+o(1))x(log log x)^2
+   <= sum_{3<=n<=x}log #M_n
+   <= (1+o(1))x log x log log x.
+
+The hypotheses log R(n)=o(omega(n)) almost everywhere and
+
+   sum_{n<=x}omega(n)log R(n)=o(x(log log x)^2)
+
+would respectively imply the desired normal order and matching mean order, but they are stated only as sufficient hypotheses H1 and H2. No positive evidence for them is claimed. Indeed, the published Bugeaud-Luca-Mignotte-Siksek conjecture omega(F_n) >> log n for composite n would make both H1 and H2 false on a positive-density set.
+
+5. Exact-rank arithmetic estimates. If A*(n) is the maximum of 1 and a(d) over divisors d|n having at least one full prime-power coordinate relative to n, then
+
+   sum_{3<=n<=x}omega(n)log R(n)
+   =sum_{3<=n<=x}omega(n)log A*(n)+O(x log log x).
+
+Thus aggregation of several exact ranks into one support window costs only O(x log log x); the unresolved mean term is genuinely the visible single-rank maximum. If phi_E is Euler's totient and varphi=(1+sqrt(5))/2, the fibotomic integer
+
+   Ffrak_d=product_{r|d}F_r^{mu(d/r)}=Psi_d(1)
+
+is a positive integer divisible by product_{p in Pi_alpha(d)}p. For k>=0, m=floor(k/2), and
+
+   H_d(k)=k log(2d/3)+2 log(m!)+(k-2m)log(m+1),
+
+there is an absolute C_F such that H_d(a(d))<=phi_E(d)log varphi+C_F for every d>=3, with the corresponding finitely weighted inequality. Uniformly,
+
+   a(d) <= ((log varphi)/2+o(1)) phi_E(d)/log d,
+   A*(n) <= ((log varphi)/2+o(1)) phi_E(n)/log n.
+
+The paper expressly says these pointwise estimates are far too large to control W(n) at the required scale and do not supply moving exact-fiber dispersion.
+
+6. A sharp but abstract limitation of cumulative data. For a labelled k-set V and weights w_S>=1 on all nonempty S, let
+
+   Z_V(w)=sum_{C in MinCov(V)} product_{S in C}w_S,
+   A=sum_{empty!=S subseteq V}w_S.
+
+Then Z_V(w)>=C_k+A-(2^k-1), sharply for every A>=2^k-1; for k>=3 equality uniquely concentrates all excess weight on the full support V. For odd squarefree n this gives
+
+   #{squarefree m in M_n}>=C_k+omega(F_n)-(2^k-1).
+
+The equality profile is an abstract information-theoretic extremizer, not a profile proved realizable by Fibonacci exact-rank primes. Thus omega(F_n)=sum_{d|n}a(d) alone cannot provide the dispersion needed for W(n).
+
+7. Prime inverse dynamics. For every integer y>=7, y!=12, there are pairwise distinct primes p_1,p_2,... with p_0=y and alpha(p_r)=p_{r-1}; they may be chosen with p_r<E_r(y), where E_0(t)=t and E_{r+1}(t)=varphi^{E_r(t)}. Consequently every fixed-point basin Omega_x with fixed x>5 contains infinitely many primes, quantitatively at least log* X-O_x(1) below X, and for every k>=1 there are infinitely many primes of fixed-point order k, with the explicit lower bound stated in the manuscript. These conclusions answer two questions of FitzGibbons-Javaheri-Miller-Verga in the stronger prime form.
+
+What is imported, and what is actually ours
+
+The following are imported and must not be credited to us: Fibonacci rank divisibility and strong divisibility (Wall and Vinson); alpha(lcm(u,v))=lcm(alpha(u),alpha(v)) in the Fibonacci case (Luca-Tron, with Renault for the more general sequence); classical prime-power rank lifting and valuation formulas (Wall/Lengyel and related sources); existence of an exact-rank prime for every d>=3 except d=6,12 (Carmichael and Bilu-Hanrot-Voutier, with the small ranks handled separately); Stroinski's alpha-contraction and the resulting Mobius first-appearance formulas; Hearne-Wagner's minimal finite-set covers and their exact enumeration; Wagner's 1978 minimal multiplicative covers of an integer; labelled split-graph asymptotics of Bender-Richmond-Wormald/Troyka; fibotomic definitions and factorization history from Webb-Parberry, Levy, and later Byer et al.; Marques's classification of the fixed points; and Luca-Tron's theorem that every alpha-orbit reaches a fixed point.
+
+Our claim is narrower: the Fibonacci exact-fiber realization of Wagner's cover skeleton; replacing deletion of a prime label by lowering a prime-power rank label; the unique correspondence with all divisibility-minimal exact preimages; the canonical squarefree exact-fiber slice and its exact a(n_S)-weighted count; the explicit separation of the support entropy from the unresolved exact-rank multiplicities; the deaggregation and fibotomic upper estimates in the form stated above; and the sharp abstract warning that cumulative primitive-prime mass cannot replace exact-rank dispersion. The inverse-ray corollaries appear to be new statements, but their entire prime-existence mechanism is imported and they do not use the weighted-cover machinery.
+
+Priority position, stated against our interest
+
+The closest structural predecessor is not merely generic set-cover theory but Carl G. Wagner, "Minimal multiplicative covers of an integer," Discrete Mathematics 24 (1978), 87-94. Wagner directly studies divisor tuples with lcm n whose lcm falls when any component is deleted. In the prime-atom case our witness condition is exactly Wagner's condition after forgetting order. The genuinely additional case is the nontrivial lowered label of a Fibonacci prime-power ladder. Hearne-Wagner already enumerated minimal set covers; the minimal-transversal formulation is standard hypergraph duality, and its associated split-graph formulation is also standard. Therefore neither the cover skeleton nor the words "minimal transversal" are an independent combinatorial breakthrough here.
+
+The nearest arithmetic literature identified in the audits includes Kiss on primitive divisors and averages involving ranks of primes, Stroinski on alpha-contractions and cumulative counts, Sanna and Cera Da Conceicao on fixed rank-divisibility densities (and Sanna, under GRH, on fixed indices of appearance), and work on primitive divisor size. None found in the bounded searches controls the simultaneous growing exact fibers a(n_S) over the divisor lattice of a typical n. Database non-detection is not proof of priority.
+
+The Bugeaud-Luca-Mignotte-Siksek conjecture omega(F_n)>>log n is not solved here and, rather than supporting our sufficient maximum-window hypotheses, predicts their failure. The paper does not solve the Cubre-Rouse problem of infinitely many primes p with alpha(p)=p+1, Wall's/Wall-Sun-Sun problem, Granville's odd-valuation primitive-divisor conjecture in the Fibonacci case, or the general output-polynomial hypergraph transversal problem. Its canonical hypergraphs are a special coordinate-star class. The current almost-all weighted-dispersion interface is a problem isolated by this paper, not a previously named open problem that can be advertised as settled.
+
+The two FitzGibbons-Javaheri-Miller-Verga questions were checked against the final 2025 paper, and no prior resolution was found in searches through 15 August 2026; however, that is a bounded literature audit, not proof that no unindexed or unpublished resolution exists. More importantly for tier, the proof is a direct iteration of classical exact-rank prime existence. A previous assessment correctly said that it is a new and recordable corollary/application, not a new primitive-divisor theorem, prime construction, distribution result, or sieve mechanism, and it cannot by itself carry the paper at a higher tier.
+
+Machine-verification evidence and its strict evidentiary limit
+
+The supplied exact-arithmetic battery used Python 3.10.11 and SymPy 1.13.1 with an archived factorization table for F_n, n<=210. Direct divisor/rank enumeration through n=60 gave 59/59 set equalities for both B_n versus the upper-fiber formula and M_n versus the witness construction. Structural checks on all 208 layers 3<=n<=210 reported 208/208 rank-pure membership checks, 104/104 odd-layer full-cover realizations, 208/208 rank-window, support-spectrum, refined-bound, fibotomic-entropy, and radical-divisibility checks, and zero failures or counterexamples. An independent squarefree verifier gave 127/127 squarefree-slice set equalities, comparing 427 minimal generators, and checked 36 small equality profiles for the weighted sharpness theorem. It also verified the strict n=91 ladder example, the prime inverse-ray prefix 7<-13<-233<-139801, the exceptional path 7->8->6->12, exact cover counts for small k, and the reported asymptotic combinatorial ratios through k=80. Unit tests, reproduction commands, archived outputs, a 15-entry SHA-256 manifest, and independent legacy scripts are supplied.
+
+These numerical batteries are sanity checks and counterexample searches only. They never substitute for proof, do not establish any infinite statement, do not prove the normal-order or mean-order claims, do not establish H1, H2, or weighted-cover dispersion, and do not show that the abstract extremal weight profile is Fibonacci-realizable.
+
+Your previous verdict, including the adverse parts
+
+On the earlier version you recommended "reject and invite a fully reconstructed resubmission," explicitly not an ordinary major revision. You found a publishable mathematical core and no concrete counterexample to the witness-cover classification, canonical squarefree slice, inverse rays, or weighted extremal proposition. But you held that the submitted paper did not meet even The Fibonacci Quarterly's acceptance threshold because (i) the refined upper bounds depended on prime-power lifting, the prime-ladder alternative, and unique ladder-slot results that were only in an unavailable supplement, so major claims were not independently verifiable; and (ii) the priority narrative omitted Wagner 1978, the closest structural predecessor. You also objected that several imported results lacked first-source attribution; the labelled/unlabelled cover-asymptotic bridge was incomplete; the paper disclosed only half of the conflict between H1/H2 and the Bugeaud-Luca-Mignotte-Siksek conjecture; the fibotomic proof needed self-contained intermediate statements and historical attribution; examples and reproducibility packaging were inadequate; and the inverse-ray result had been given more prominence than its fully imported proof mechanism warranted. You judged the inverse rays a worthwhile direct corollary, not a core prime-existence advance, and the fixed-total-weight extremum correct but elementary and supporting rather than a major innovation. You said that, after the two structural defects and the other presentation issues were repaired, the reconstructed core would in principle clear The Fibonacci Quarterly, and that you would not then reject it merely as incremental.
+
+The present main.pdf is the reconstruction: it moves the lifting/dichotomy/slot proofs into numbered statements in the main paper, gives a formal Wagner comparison, corrects first-source attributions, directly proves the labelled comparison needed for log C_k, labels H1/H2 only as sufficient hypotheses and states that BLMS contradicts both, makes the fibotomic step self-contained, lowers the prominence of the inverse-ray application, adds the requested worked examples, and supplies the verification archive. We are not asking you to reconsider the lower landing level. We are asking what addition could raise the ceiling.
+
+Please answer the following in this exact order.
+
+1. Name the single most valuable NEW RESULT that would move this paper up one tier. Give an explicit theorem statement with all hypotheses and quantifiers, not a topic or research direction. State the named open problem, existing theorem, or standard object to which it attaches, with proposer/authors and a precise literature location. If more than one candidate is genuinely competitive, rank them, but still identify one first choice.
+
+2. For each candidate, decide whether it is REACHABLE FROM THE MACHINERY ALREADY IN THIS PAPER. Identify by name the existing lemmas, constructions, estimates, or sectors that would feed the proof. Separate the remaining work into (i) deductions plausibly available from those ingredients and (ii) a genuinely new ingredient. If the missing ingredient is moving-Chebotarev uniformity, a sieve estimate, p-adic information, distribution of a(d), a least-exact-rank-prime bound, a realizability construction, or something comparably external, say so plainly. If the theorem is not reachable from the present machinery, do not present it as a target; state what machinery would be needed instead.
+
+3. Rank the candidates by success probability multiplied by tier impact. Give the success probability as a numerical percentage, not an adjective, and separately score the tier impact so that the product is auditable. Distinguish probability that the mathematics is true, probability that this paper's machinery can prove it, and probability that its addition actually changes the referee's tier assessment.
+
+4. For the leading candidate, say which of the four standard tier-raising levers genuinely applies, and exactly how: (a) settling a named open problem in the literature; (b) proving a theorem about the field's own standard objects, such as alpha^{-1}(n), primitive divisors, or standard hypergraph transversals, rather than only about a class defined by this paper; (c) removing a hypothesis from an existing theorem, especially one currently assumed for convenience; (d) proving a sharpness or matching-bound theorem showing that an existing estimate cannot be improved. More than one may apply, but do not count a merely formal reformulation as a lever.
+
+5. Assume the leading new theorem has been proved and integrated optimally. What is the strongest objection a referee at the higher tier would still raise? State the objection in its hardest plausible form and state the exact mathematical result, proof ingredient, priority evidence, or restructuring that would resolve it.
+
+Constraints on your answer
+
+Do not answer the tier question by recommending a lower-tier journal. We already know where the reconstructed paper lands as it stands; we are asking what would move it up.
+
+Do not propose adding more theorems of the same internally generated kind. An earlier round of our work mechanically accumulated results and moved nothing. Six of our seven papers were then found to have priority omissions, because formally new statements composed from standard equivalences carry little independent priority. A new support taxonomy, another low-coordinate classification, another restatement as a standard combinatorial object, another finite table, or another corollary of classical exact-rank existence is not a satisfactory answer unless it settles a specific external theorem-level target whose prior status you verify.
+
+Do not repeat the already-added prime inverse-ray result as the tier-raising target. It has been proved, audited, and incorporated, and its limited methodological novelty is already understood.
+
+If the honest conclusion is that no result reachable from the present machinery can raise this paper, say so plainly and identify the structural feature that caps it. In particular, distinguish a ceiling caused by the machinery being intrafiber and combinatorial while higher-tier questions require inter-index arithmetic distribution, small-prime control, p-adic input, or sieve theory. A clear, well-argued ceiling will be acted on; vague encouragement will not.
+
+A hard, specific negative is more useful than a soft positive. Every objection must be paired with what would resolve it. Every proposed theorem must be paired with a falsification test: say what calculation, counterexample pattern, literature hit, or failed intermediate estimate would show that the route is unsound. Flag every mathematical or priority step you have not independently verified.

+one has, on odd n,
+
+   log #R_n^rp=(log 2)k^2/4+log W(n)+O(k),  W(n)>=1.
+
+Consequently the desired almost-all formula
+
+   log #M_n ~ (log 2)/4 (log log n)^2
+
+would necessarily require log W(n)=o((log log n)^2) outside o(x) odd integers n<=x. This condition is not known to be sufficient, because ladder and other non-rank-pure covers must also be bounded; already 169 lies in M_91 but outside R_91^rp.
+
+4. Unconditional counting bounds, with the unresolved arithmetic term displayed rather than hidden. Let R(n) be the maximum of 1 and the cardinalities of the atomic families A_{I,J}(n). For n>=3, M_n is nonempty. If k=omega(n)>=3 and r=floor(k/2), then
+
+   (2^r-1)^{k-r} <= #M_n
+   <= sum_{s=1}^k binom(k,s)(2R(n))^s 2^{s(k-s)}.
+
+Therefore, uniformly when omega(n)->infinity,
+
+   (log 2)omega(n)^2/4-O(1)
+   <= log #M_n
+   <= (log 2)omega(n)^2/4+omega(n)log R(n)+O(omega(n)).
+
+The paper does not claim an unconditional asymptotic or an exact quadratic coefficient for the full M_n. It also proves the coarser bounds #M_n<=n^{omega(n)}, limsup log(#M_n)log log n/(log n)^2<=1, and
+
+   ((log 2)/4+o(1))x(log log x)^2
+   <= sum_{3<=n<=x}log #M_n
+   <= (1+o(1))x log x log log x.
+
+The hypotheses log R(n)=o(omega(n)) almost everywhere and
+
+   sum_{n<=x}omega(n)log R(n)=o(x(log log x)^2)
+
+would respectively imply the desired normal order and matching mean order, but they are stated only as sufficient hypotheses H1 and H2. No positive evidence for them is claimed. Indeed, the published Bugeaud-Luca-Mignotte-Siksek conjecture omega(F_n) >> log n for composite n would make both H1 and H2 false on a positive-density set.
+
+5. Exact-rank arithmetic estimates. If A*(n) is the maximum of 1 and a(d) over divisors d|n having at least one full prime-power coordinate relative to n, then
+
+   sum_{3<=n<=x}omega(n)log R(n)
+   =sum_{3<=n<=x}omega(n)log A*(n)+O(x log log x).
+
+Thus aggregation of several exact ranks into one support window costs only O(x log log x); the unresolved mean term is genuinely the visible single-rank maximum. If phi_E is Euler's totient and varphi=(1+sqrt(5))/2, the fibotomic integer
+
+   Ffrak_d=product_{r|d}F_r^{mu(d/r)}=Psi_d(1)
+
+is a positive integer divisible by product_{p in Pi_alpha(d)}p. For k>=0, m=floor(k/2), and
+
+   H_d(k)=k log(2d/3)+2 log(m!)+(k-2m)log(m+1),
+
+there is an absolute C_F such that H_d(a(d))<=phi_E(d)log varphi+C_F for every d>=3, with the corresponding finitely weighted inequality. Uniformly,
+
+   a(d) <= ((log varphi)/2+o(1)) phi_E(d)/log d,
+   A*(n) <= ((log varphi)/2+o(1)) phi_E(n)/log n.
+
+The paper expressly says these pointwise estimates are far too large to control W(n) at the required scale and do not supply moving exact-fiber dispersion.
+
+6. A sharp but abstract limitation of cumulative data. For a labelled k-set V and weights w_S>=1 on all nonempty S, let
+
+   Z_V(w)=sum_{C in MinCov(V)} product_{S in C}w_S,
+   A=sum_{empty!=S subseteq V}w_S.
+
+Then Z_V(w)>=C_k+A-(2^k-1), sharply for every A>=2^k-1; for k>=3 equality uniquely concentrates all excess weight on the full support V. For odd squarefree n this gives
+
+   #{squarefree m in M_n}>=C_k+omega(F_n)-(2^k-1).
+
+The equality profile is an abstract information-theoretic extremizer, not a profile proved realizable by Fibonacci exact-rank primes. Thus omega(F_n)=sum_{d|n}a(d) alone cannot provide the dispersion needed for W(n).
+
+7. Prime inverse dynamics. For every integer y>=7, y!=12, there are pairwise distinct primes p_1,p_2,... with p_0=y and alpha(p_r)=p_{r-1}; they may be chosen with p_r<E_r(y), where E_0(t)=t and E_{r+1}(t)=varphi^{E_r(t)}. Consequently every fixed-point basin Omega_x with fixed x>5 contains infinitely many primes, quantitatively at least log* X-O_x(1) below X, and for every k>=1 there are infinitely many primes of fixed-point order k, with the explicit lower bound stated in the manuscript. These conclusions answer two questions of FitzGibbons-Javaheri-Miller-Verga in the stronger prime form.
+
+What is imported, and what is actually ours
+
+The following are imported and must not be credited to us: Fibonacci rank divisibility and strong divisibility (Wall and Vinson); alpha(lcm(u,v))=lcm(alpha(u),alpha(v)) in the Fibonacci case (Luca-Tron, with Renault for the more general sequence); classical prime-power rank lifting and valuation formulas (Wall/Lengyel and related sources); existence of an exact-rank prime for every d>=3 except d=6,12 (Carmichael and Bilu-Hanrot-Voutier, with the small ranks handled separately); Stroinski's alpha-contraction and the resulting Mobius first-appearance formulas; Hearne-Wagner's minimal finite-set covers and their exact enumeration; Wagner's 1978 minimal multiplicative covers of an integer; labelled split-graph asymptotics of Bender-Richmond-Wormald/Troyka; fibotomic definitions and factorization history from Webb-Parberry, Levy, and later Byer et al.; Marques's classification of the fixed points; and Luca-Tron's theorem that every alpha-orbit reaches a fixed point.
+
+Our claim is narrower: the Fibonacci exact-fiber realization of Wagner's cover skeleton; replacing deletion of a prime label by lowering a prime-power rank label; the unique correspondence with all divisibility-minimal exact preimages; the canonical squarefree exact-fiber slice and its exact a(n_S)-weighted count; the explicit separation of the support entropy from the unresolved exact-rank multiplicities; the deaggregation and fibotomic upper estimates in the form stated above; and the sharp abstract warning that cumulative primitive-prime mass cannot replace exact-rank dispersion. The inverse-ray corollaries appear to be new statements, but their entire prime-existence mechanism is imported and they do not use the weighted-cover machinery.
+
+Priority position, stated against our interest
+
+The closest structural predecessor is not merely generic set-cover theory but Carl G. Wagner, "Minimal multiplicative covers of an integer," Discrete Mathematics 24 (1978), 87-94. Wagner directly studies divisor tuples with lcm n whose lcm falls when any component is deleted. In the prime-atom case our witness condition is exactly Wagner's condition after forgetting order. The genuinely additional case is the nontrivial lowered label of a Fibonacci prime-power ladder. Hearne-Wagner already enumerated minimal set covers; the minimal-transversal formulation is standard hypergraph duality, and its associated split-graph formulation is also standard. Therefore neither the cover skeleton nor the words "minimal transversal" are an independent combinatorial breakthrough here.
+
+The nearest arithmetic literature identified in the audits includes Kiss on primitive divisors and averages involving ranks of primes, Stroinski on alpha-contractions and cumulative counts, Sanna and Cera Da Conceicao on fixed rank-divisibility densities (and Sanna, under GRH, on fixed indices of appearance), and work on primitive divisor size. None found in the bounded searches controls the simultaneous growing exact fibers a(n_S) over the divisor lattice of a typical n. Database non-detection is not proof of priority.
+
+The Bugeaud-Luca-Mignotte-Siksek conjecture omega(F_n)>>log n is not solved here and, rather than supporting our sufficient maximum-window hypotheses, predicts their failure. The paper does not solve the Cubre-Rouse problem of infinitely many primes p with alpha(p)=p+1, Wall's/Wall-Sun-Sun problem, Granville's odd-valuation primitive-divisor conjecture in the Fibonacci case, or the general output-polynomial hypergraph transversal problem. Its canonical hypergraphs are a special coordinate-star class. The current almost-all weighted-dispersion interface is a problem isolated by this paper, not a previously named open problem that can be advertised as settled.
+
+The two FitzGibbons-Javaheri-Miller-Verga questions were checked against the final 2025 paper, and no prior resolution was found in searches through 15 August 2026; however, that is a bounded literature audit, not proof that no unindexed or unpublished resolution exists. More importantly for tier, the proof is a direct iteration of classical exact-rank prime existence. A previous assessment correctly said that it is a new and recordable corollary/application, not a new primitive-divisor theorem, prime construction, distribution result, or sieve mechanism, and it cannot by itself carry the paper at a higher tier.
+
+Machine-verification evidence and its strict evidentiary limit
+
+The supplied exact-arithmetic battery used Python 3.10.11 and SymPy 1.13.1 with an archived factorization table for F_n, n<=210. Direct divisor/rank enumeration through n=60 gave 59/59 set equalities for both B_n versus the upper-fiber formula and M_n versus the witness construction. Structural checks on all 208 layers 3<=n<=210 reported 208/208 rank-pure membership checks, 104/104 odd-layer full-cover realizations, 208/208 rank-window, support-spectrum, refined-bound, fibotomic-entropy, and radical-divisibility checks, and zero failures or counterexamples. An independent squarefree verifier gave 127/127 squarefree-slice set equalities, comparing 427 minimal generators, and checked 36 small equality profiles for the weighted sharpness theorem. It also verified the strict n=91 ladder example, the prime inverse-ray prefix 7<-13<-233<-139801, the exceptional path 7->8->6->12, exact cover counts for small k, and the reported asymptotic combinatorial ratios through k=80. Unit tests, reproduction commands, archived outputs, a 15-entry SHA-256 manifest, and independent legacy scripts are supplied.
+
+These numerical batteries are sanity checks and counterexample searches only. They never substitute for proof, do not establish any infinite statement, do not prove the normal-order or mean-order claims, do not establish H1, H2, or weighted-cover dispersion, and do not show that the abstract extremal weight profile is Fibonacci-realizable.
+
+Your previous verdict, including the adverse parts
+
+On the earlier version you recommended "reject and invite a fully reconstructed resubmission," explicitly not an ordinary major revision. You found a publishable mathematical core and no concrete counterexample to the witness-cover classification, canonical squarefree slice, inverse rays, or weighted extremal proposition. But you held that the submitted paper did not meet even The Fibonacci Quarterly's acceptance threshold because (i) the refined upper bounds depended on prime-power lifting, the prime-ladder alternative, and unique ladder-slot results that were only in an unavailable supplement, so major claims were not independently verifiable; and (ii) the priority narrative omitted Wagner 1978, the closest structural predecessor. You also objected that several imported results lacked first-source attribution; the labelled/unlabelled cover-asymptotic bridge was incomplete; the paper disclosed only half of the conflict between H1/H2 and the Bugeaud-Luca-Mignotte-Siksek conjecture; the fibotomic proof needed self-contained intermediate statements and historical attribution; examples and reproducibility packaging were inadequate; and the inverse-ray result had been given more prominence than its fully imported proof mechanism warranted. You judged the inverse rays a worthwhile direct corollary, not a core prime-existence advance, and the fixed-total-weight extremum correct but elementary and supporting rather than a major innovation. You said that, after the two structural defects and the other presentation issues were repaired, the reconstructed core would in principle clear The Fibonacci Quarterly, and that you would not then reject it merely as incremental.
+
+The present main.pdf is the reconstruction: it moves the lifting/dichotomy/slot proofs into numbered statements in the main paper, gives a formal Wagner comparison, corrects first-source attributions, directly proves the labelled comparison needed for log C_k, labels H1/H2 only as sufficient hypotheses and states that BLMS contradicts both, makes the fibotomic step self-contained, lowers the prominence of the inverse-ray application, adds the requested worked examples, and supplies the verification archive. We are not asking you to reconsider the lower landing level. We are asking what addition could raise the ceiling.
+

State the named open problem, existing theorem, or standard object to which it attaches, with proposer/authors and a precise literature location. If more than one candidate is genuinely competitive, rank them, but still identify one first choice.

+2. For each candidate, decide whether it is REACHABLE FROM THE MACHINERY ALREADY IN THIS PAPER. Identify by name the existing lemmas, constructions, estimates, or sectors that would feed the proof. Separate the remaining work into (i) deductions plausibly available from those ingredients and (ii) a genuinely new ingredient. If the missing ingredient is moving-Chebotarev uniformity, a sieve estimate, p-adic information, distribution of a(d), a least-exact-rank-prime bound, a realizability construction, or something comparably external, say so plainly. If the theorem is not reachable from the present machinery, do not present it as a target; state what machinery would be needed instead.

Definitions and main results proved in the manuscript

Let F_0=0, F_1=1, and let alpha(q) be the least r>=1 for which q divides F_r, with alpha(1)=1. For n>=2 let B_n=alpha^{-1}(n) and let M_n be the divisibility-minimal elements of B_n; B_2=M_2 is empty. Write P(n) for the prime divisors of n, omega(n)=#P(n), Pi_alpha(d)={p prime: alpha(p)=d}, and a(d)=#Pi_alpha(d).

1. Exact classification of the minimal exact fiber. For a prime power theta=p^e set d(theta)=alpha(p^e) and d^-(theta)=alpha(p^{e-1}); call theta atomic when d^-(theta)<d(theta). If d(theta) divides n, define its full support

   T_n(theta)={ell in P(n): nu_ell(d(theta))=nu_ell(n)}

and its essential support

   E_n(theta)={ell in T_n(theta): nu_ell(d^-(theta))<nu_ell(n)}.

An n-witness cover is a finite pairwise-coprime set Theta of atomic prime powers such that every d(theta) divides n, the sets T_n(theta) cover P(n), and every theta has an essential coordinate absent from all other full supports:

   E_n(theta) is not contained in the union of T_n(eta) over eta!=theta.
